#2292. Serie B, Modena-Juve Stabia: spicca il duello tra Belotti e Di Nardo

Tornano ad affrontarsi Modena e Juve Stabia a sei mesi dalla sfida valida per il primo turno dei playoff. Il 12 maggio scorso la rovesciata di Zeroli a quattro minuti dalla fine permise alle “Vespe” di staccare il pass per la semifinale contro il Monza. Stavolta emiliani e campani si sfideranno per la 12^ giornata questo pomeriggio ore 15 al Braglia riprendendo il cammino interrotto per la seconda sosta stagionale, disputando la prima di otto partite da disputare nel giro di poco più di un mese prima della consueta interruzione di fine anno. I padroni di casa riprendono dal secondo posto in graduatoria a 23 punti a quattro lunghezze dalla capolista Palermo con il secondo miglior attacco e la seconda retroguardia meno battuta. Gli ospiti occupano il 12^ posto a 13 punti ma virtualmente sarebbero sesti dovendo scontare quattro punti di penalizzazione. Dirige il confronto Rosario Abisso di Palermo.

Come arriva il Modena - Daniele Galloppa recupera Nador e Consiglio, mentre Acquah si aggiunge alla corposa lista degli indisponibili completata da Caso, Tonoli, Sersanti, Manquant, Stenio Zanetti. Il modulo potrebbe variare dal 3-4-2-1 al 4-3-2-1 in questo inizio di stagione i canarini hanno mostrato molta versatilità sotto questo punto di vista. Ipotizzando la prima soluzione Folino potrebbe affiancare Adorni e Nieling davanti a protezione di Chichizola. Imputato e Azzi sulle fasce affiancati da Santoro e Brugman. Ambrosino ed Olzer a supporto di Belotti in leggero vantaggio su Montevago.

Come arriva la Juve Stabia - Sull’altra sponda Pietro De Giorgio deve fare a meno di Pietrangeli, Scudieri e Sandrucci. Si affiderà al 4-3-3 con Vukovic fra i pali, Bettella e Bellich al centro della difesa supportati da Maggioni e Douglas sistemati sulle fasce. Artioli in cabina di regia mentre per le mezzali si annunciano ballottaggi tra Battistella e Kumi e fra Pierobon e Karic. Nessun dubbio invece sulla presenza al centro dell’attacco di Di Nardo supportato ai lati da Maisto e Sibilli con Candellone e Patanè pronti a subentrare in corso d’opera.

Intervenuto ai microfoni di TMW Radio, Massimo Bonanni, ex calciatore, ha parlato così dei rinnovi di Dimarco, Thuram e Calhanoglu:

"Sono tutti e tre giocatori forti, che dovrebbero essere rinnovati. Per Calhanoglu sarebbe uno degli ultimi contratti, ma rimane molto forte, ancora in grado di determinare. "A bocce ferme la partita più bella è quella dell'Inter a Bergamo. Poi anche le altre hanno gare relativamente difficili, dato che anche la sfida del Milan contro il Frosinone potrebbe essere una trappola, così come la Lazio con il Lecce. Atalanta-Inter però è sicuramente la più bella del fine settimana. Con Sarri a Bergamo si è passati da un tipo di strategia tattica ad un approccio completamente diverso. Quello di Gasperini e Palladino era meno razionale e molto più fisico. Ad oggi i nerazzurri sono una delle più grandi delusioni del campionato, mentre negli ultimi anni era sempre stata in alto con una continuità incredibile. Con una sconfitta contro l'Inter si rischia di finire a -13 dalla zona Champions. Non so quale sia il pensiero della società, però le difficoltà ci sono. Quella distanza dal quarto posto è un campanello importante".

#2289. Napoli, Edo De Laurentiis: “Ho commesso degli errori, ma lavoro ogni giorno per essere all’altezza del mio ruolo”

E’ andato in scena la prima edizione del premio “Eccellenza Calcistica Italiana”, riconoscimento dedicato alle figure che hanno lasciato il segno nel panorama calcistico nazionale. Tra i vincitori figura anche il vicepresidente della SSC Napoli, Edoardo De Laurentiis, premiato per i meriti conseguiti nell’area della Governance: “E’ per me un grande onore ricevere questo riconoscimento, ma la gioia è doppia per averlo condiviso con mio padre, che è il mio mentore e la mia guida. Ho mosso i primi passi in questo mondo quando ero ancora molto giovane, ho commesso diversi sbagli, ma mi impegno quotidiano per crescere ed essere all’altezza del mio ruolo”.

Successivamente, a margine della cerimonia di premiazione, il vicepresidente ha aggiunto: “La chiave è proseguire giorno dopo giorno con costanza, facendo gruppo. La coesione è fondamentale per raggiungere gli obiettivi prefissati. Diventerà la futura casa del Napoli (riferendosi al nuovo centro sportivo), un luogo in cui far crescere i giovani, costruire il nostro vivaio e scoprire nuovi talenti. Abbiamo vissuto tantissime esperienze entusiasmanti, dall’era Spalletti fino a Conte. La più grande è stata osservare la squadra capace di conquistare costantemente traguardi prestigiosi e di mantenersi sempre ad altissimi livelli”.

#2288. Lazio e Roma e il record da battere in questo turno

La capolista affronta il Lecce, fermo a 10 punti; la squadra di Gasperini trova il Parma, a quota otto.

Lazio e Roma possono allungare a 12 giornate il loro primato congiunto di imbattibilità nel prossimo turno di Serie A. Dopo la sosta per le Nazionali, il campionato 2026-27 riprende con la dodicesima giornata, in programma dal 21 al 23 novembre 2026. Le due squadre capitoline giocheranno entrambe sabato 21 novembre.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Come riportato da Opta, Lazio e Roma sono rimaste entrambe imbattute nelle prime 11 giornate per la prima volta nella storia delle formazioni romane nella massima serie. Il risultato raggiunto prima della pausa può quindi essere migliorato nel prossimo turno: se nessuna delle due perderà, la serie condivisa salirà a 12 giornate.

Le partite delle due squadre sono concentrate nello spazio di poche ore. Alle 15 la Lazio ospiterà il Lecce all’Olimpico; alle 18 toccherà alla Roma, impegnata sul campo del Parma. Saranno questi i due incontri da cui dipenderà la possibilità di prolungare il primato congiunto.

#2284. De Laurentiis: “Film sul Centenario? De Giovanni ha scritto la sceneggiatura, Amazon lo porterà nel mondo

Aurelio De Laurentiis, presidente del Napoli, è stato tra i protagonisti della prima edizione del premio “Eccellenza Calcistica Italiana”, manifestazione dedicata alle personalità che si sono distinte nel panorama calcistico nazionale. L’evento si è svolto a Villa Campolieto, a Ercolano, dove il patron azzurro ha ricevuto un riconoscimento nella categoria Fair Play, che vedeva tra i finalisti anche Andrea Belotti e Luca Percassi. Nel corso del suo intervento, De Laurentiis ha parlato anche del film dedicato al Centenario del Napoli: “La sceneggiatura del film del centenario la scrive il qui presente Maurizio de Giovanni. Il calcio in Inghilterra è nato con gli aristocratici. Non era un fatto popolare. Arrivarono a Napoli e iniziarono a giocare con gli scugnizzi. Il 1922 sembrerebbe la vera data, poi procrastinata al 1926 per la nascita del Napoli. Che non si chiama Società Sportiva, ma Calcio Napoli. Questo film è venuto talmente bene che una piattaforma importante come Amazon lo porterà nel mondo. Quando abbiamo vinto lo Scudetto con Conte, ho fatto un film e sono andato a Los Angeles: presi questo cinema e ho fatto questa anteprima dove sono arrivati questi tifosi dall’America. C’erano donne emozionatissime che dicevano di non capire nulla di calcio ma che si sono comunque fatte trascinare. Ho scoperto che a San Pedro ci sono 30mila tifosi del Napoli legati all’isola d’Ischia”.

#2281. Man United transfer news: Red Devils eye Ligue 1 wonderkid to replace JJ Gabriel

By Axel Clody | 19 Nov 2026 06:31

Manchester United have reportedly stepped up their search for a successor to English gem JJ Gabriel, with the Red Devils making the signing of a Ligue 1 prodigy their priority.

It was an open secret, but it is now official. In a message posted on his Instagram account, JJ Gabriel confirmed his departure from Manchester United.

Unwilling to sign his first professional contract with the Red Devils, because of a lack of recognition that has prevented him from playing a single game for the first team, the 16-year-old hopeful has no shortage of suitors for the next stage of a career that is only just beginning.

Wanted by Paris Saint-Germain, Barcelona and Bayern Munich, JJ Gabriel is currently tipped to join Real Madrid in a deal estimated at around £12.8m (€15m). For their part, the Red Devils, now managed by Argentine boss Mauricio Pochettino, have probably anticipated the loss of their attacking midfielder for some time.

He scored 34 goals in 35 games in all competitions with the various youth teams without ever breaking into the first team.

Behind the scenes, the Old Trafford club appear to have already made a decision on a successor for JJ Gabriel.

According to information from ManUtd Addicts, relayed by the specialist website Africa Foot, Manchester United want to sign a Ligue 1 prodigy: Mezian Mesloub. Very advanced talks have reportedly taken place over the Lens player’s arrival in England.

While no deadline has been mentioned, the Portuguese hopeful could reportedly be signed by Manchester United before being loaned straight back to Lens. The English club would thus get their hands on the player ahead of the other suitors, while allowing Lens to keep him for some time yet.

A product of the Gaillette academy, Mezian Mesloub made his first-team debut in the 2025-26 season, in the 33rd Ligue 1 game against Nantes (1-0), when he made his mark by scoring the only goal of the match shortly after coming on.

But it is above all this season, 2026-27, that his playing time has increased sharply: eight games, including five in Ligue 1, one start and one assist in Lens’ only Champions League win so far, away at Slavia Prague (3-2).

While it is probably inconceivable for Lens to part with Mesloub so quickly, his progress and contract situation inevitably attract interest.

Wanted by Benfica and PSV Eindhoven, the Martigues native signed his first professional contract last summer, until June 2030, with last season’s Ligue 1 runners-up to Paris Saint-Germain. That gives Lens hope of one day making a very good sale for a player whose market value rose from just £425,000 (€500,000) to £6.8m (€8m) between June and September 2026.

#2279. Raffaele De Leonardis: “La Zes, il laghetto, le fake news: tutti i retroscena della trattativa con De Laurentiis per il centro sportivo a Qualiano”

A Radio Napoli Centrale, nel corso di Un Calcio alla Radio è intervenuto Raffaele De Leonardis (sindaco di Qualiano): “Quali sono i progetti da affiancare al centro sportivo del Napoli? Dopo la firma ho già pensato a cosa fare attorno alla zona. Quella sarà una cittadella dello sport, perché affianco ai terreni dove sorgerà il centro sportivo c’è uno stadio, ci sono campi da tennis e abbiamo in animo un palazzetto dello sport e una piscina. Oggi c’è la parte più difficile, quella della burocrazia e infrastrutture. In poco tempo che mi rimane come sindaco mi metterò a disposizione. Quante volte è saltata la trattativa con il Napoli e perché? La prima volta è saltata per il prezzo d’acquisto. Il Presidente De Laurentiis doveva valutare l’investimento. I primi terreni con destinazione urbanistica si parlava di 100 euro a metro quadro. Non c’era bisogno della Zes. Servivano 20 milioni di euro. Non era nelle disponibilità e intenzioni della società. Perché poi bisognava costruire il centro. E comparando con il Viola Park, finanziato dal 20% del Coni, ci fu un primo stop. Successivamente, la Zes paventava di superare vincoli urbanistici, allora si è cominciato a parlare di terreni agricoli. E quindi si è riallacciato il rapporto perché c’erano terreni che rientravano nei desideri della SSC Napoli. C’è stata una visita, sotto la pioggia, il famoso 30 marzo dell’anno dello Scudetto. Il Presidente del Napoli mi invitò allo stadio Maradona, vidi Massimo Boldi, c’era Serena Brancale, c’era De Martino. Quanti vaffa mi sono preso dal Presidente? Abbastanza (sorride ndr). Nell’ultima riunione c’era Chiavelli, c’erano Testa, Discepolo, c’era anche Edo e lì ho capito che forse c’eravamo. Se De Laurentiis si è messo in auto per vedere le distanze dalle stazioni ai terreni? Sì, la prima volta che venne a visitare i terreni prima di Napoli-Milan fece questo percorso in auto. Il Presidente De Laurentiis chiedeva quanto distasse dalla stazione di Afragola, ed erano circa 20 minuti, dalla stazione di Ponte Riccio al centro sono 10 minuti a piedi e da Varcaturo o zone residenziali dei giocatori sono 8 minuti di auto e dallo stadio sono 20 minuti con la tangenziale. Dall’aeroporto ancora meno, 12-13 minuti. De Laurentiis percepiva che la posizione era buona. Fece però un passo indietro in merito alla storia dei rifiuti tossici, si era spaventato, ma non era vero.

Quanto mi hanno dato fastidio le fake news attorno a questo? No, sono il Sindaco di Qualiano, ne sento tante. Però penso che ci sono solo pensieri in negativo. Ma pensiamo anche in positivo. Tutte queste problematiche potrebbero nascere, magari con la Zes. Ma noi che ci guadagniamo? Niente! C’è uno strumento di legge, sono stati fatti i migliori investimenti, noi stiamo sperando che non venga approvato? De Laurentiis ha firmato un atto notarile, un preliminare condizionato e se arriva l’autorizzazione, si trasforma automaticamente in definitivo. Lui che se ne fa di un terreno agricolo!? La Zes ha sette linee guida. Una delle linee riguarda l’attività produttiva per quanto riguarda turismo e sport. Noi non ci opporremo, perché sarebbe da folli. Chiederemo le infrastrutture che dovranno essere realizzate e aiuteremo il Napoli a realizzarle. Noi stiamo rispettando la legge. Se il prof Discepolo presenta il progetto definitivo in 10-15 giorni e partono i 90 giorni della Zes, seguiremo il countdown. Sentivo parlare di un’autorizzazione preliminare per il muro di cinta, con autorizzazione dei proprietari. Se ci sarà un laghetto? Non so se è un vezzo, ma servirà per irrigare i campi. Sarà a mo’ di cisterna. Se ci saranno hotel? Ci saranno sicuramente le stanze per i giocatori. De Laurentiis tiene tanto alla cantera, penso che farà anche un convitto per i giovani”.

#2278. Man United news: Neville backs Dalot and names three defenders to sell before him

By Axel Clody | 19 Nov 2026 06:43

Diogo Dalot has become one of the targets of Manchester United supporters’ dissatisfaction, but Gary Neville believes the Portuguese full-back is far from being one of the squad’s main problems.

The former Manchester United captain came out in defence of his fellow right-back after the boos Dalot received at Old Trafford and said he would get rid of other players before parting with the number two.

The criticism comes at a time of instability for Manchester United, who are struggling to find consistency in the Premier League and are again facing questions over the individual performances of their players. Dalot was one of the negative protagonists in the draw with Aston Villa, particularly because of the penalty he conceded, and his substitution during the game was greeted with celebrations by the home fans.

‘I would be getting rid of some players before Dalot. Dalot got booed last week at Old Trafford and I was really surprised at that’ - the former full-back said on the ‘Stick to Football’ podcast.

In Neville’s view, Dalot is receiving excessive criticism for frequently playing out of his natural position. The Portuguese is a right-back, but has also been used on the left side, either as a full-back or wing-back, depending on the team’s needs.

The former Manchester United player stressed that this change of role requires adaptation and that Dalot shows a positive attitude in accepting the tasks given to him by the manager.

‘If you put me at left wing-back or left-back, and I am a right-back, and he is too, I would say: “No way, why are you putting me there?”’

The pundit, however, believes Dalot has shown a willingness to help the team, even when he has to play in a position different from the one in which he feels most comfortable.

‘Instead, Dalot literally sticks his hand up and says: “Go on, I’ll play there, I will do anything for the team”. I never see him shirk anything, I never look at him and think he could try harder’ - he added.

Neville’s defence does not mean the full-back is free from criticism of his performances. The Portuguese struggled against Aston Villa and conceded a penalty that contributed to the result. The former captain, however, believes individual performance needs to be analysed within the context of a squad with collective problems and players often shifted out of their usual roles.

Neville also compared Dalot with other members of Manchester United’s defensive unit and said there are players who should be sold before the Portuguese. The former full-back specifically named Noussair Mazraoui, Lisandro Martinez and Matthijs de Ligt.

‘I watch defenders very closely and, if you ask me, I would be getting rid of Mazraoui, well gone, before him, I would be getting rid of Martinez well before him, I would be getting rid of De Ligt gone before him.’

The opinion stands out because the three players named have also faced questions at different points of the season. Mazraoui has been used in different defensive roles, while Martinez has dealt with injury problems and controversy. De Ligt, meanwhile, has also been subject to the team’s fluctuations in performance and changes to the defensive set-up.

Dalot joined Manchester United in 2018 from Porto and has had different spells at the club. After a loan at Milan, he returned to Old Trafford and earned a place in the team, particularly during the 2022-23 season. Since then, he has become a regular presence, although his use on both sides of defence has fuelled debate about his best position.

While the club try to find stability, the Portuguese remains a recurring option in defence. For Neville, the pressure on the full-back should not be greater than that directed at other players who, in his assessment, have performed worse or contributed less to the team’s needs.

#2277. Significant update emerges on Southampton’s new contract talks with Tonda Eckert

Southampton stuck by head coach Tonda Eckert after the Spygate saga. And, when his punishment of a suspended ban and fine was confirmed, it soon emerged that the Saints were preparing a new contract offer to secure the boss’ future beyond the end of the 2026/27 campaign.

Now, the club have confirmed that Eckert’s new deal at St Mary’s is done. His new contract will run until 2029, marking a two-year extension on his existing terms.

Eckert was appointed as the Saints’ permanent boss in December 2025 after an impressive spell as caretaker after Will Still’s sacking. The 33-year-old had only joined as U21s head coach in the summer, but his quick ascent has seen him emerge as one of Europe’s brightest coaching prospects.

Eckert’s breakthrough has been mightily impressive, despite the fact Spygate still casts a cloud over his Southampton record.

After a string of roles in Germany and assistant manager positions at Barnsley and Genoa, Eckert’s step to become the Saints’ U21s boss was a good one for the trajectory of his career. Few saw him making such an early rise into the first-team role, but Still’s struggles and his success as caretaker meant he was quickly solidified in his place as boss.

He has averaged just under two points per game in his time as permanent head coach, and the four-point deduction his side started the season with is a thing of the past. That and a new contract for Eckert has helped lead the turmoil of Spygate in the past.

Importantly though, Eckert’s new deal will fend off potential admirers. Clubs were bound to have him on the radar as he closed on the end of his contract, but a hefty compensation fee will now have to be paid to prise him away from the South Coast club.

Spygate will be brought up at every corner by opposing fans and it will have a place in English football history forever. Eckert will face questions about it for the foreseeable, and many will still debate that the punishment he faced was not strong enough.

However, Eckert and the Saints are moving on by focusing on the long term and delivering success on the pitch. A new contract solidifies the club’s commitment to him despite the end to last season.

After losing 1-0 to Preston North End before the break, Eckert will be eager to see his side respond when they face Derby County this Saturday.

#2276. Arsenal transfer news: Gunners 'tracking' Antonio Nusa and £22m alternative as January swoop eyed

By Lewis Blain | 19 Nov 2026 07:00

Arsenal are continuing to assess their attacking options ahead of the January transfer window, with Mikel Arteta keen to add another forward to his squad.

The Premier League champions are understood to be 'tracking' RB Leipzig's Antonio Nusa and Brentford's Kevin Schade as potential targets.

Both players fit the profile of a forward who primarily operates on the left but can also provide cover across the frontline.

According to Sky Sports, the Gunners are preparing their recruitment plans for 2027 after failing to secure a marquee forward during the summer transfer window.

Nusa and Schade have emerged as two players on the club's radar, with the Gunners looking to strengthen their attacking options ahead of January.

The north London outfit previously pursued Morgan Rogers, Vinicius Junior and Julian Alvarez, but failed to agree deals for any of their preferred targets.

Arsenal are now believed to be moving on from Alvarez, who is not keen to leave Spain, while Rogers completed a £117m move to Chelsea – a fee the Gunners were unwilling to match. Vinicius, meanwhile, has committed his future to Real Madrid by signing a new contract.

With those pursuits unsuccessful, Arteta's recruitment team appear to be considering alternative profiles, with Nusa and Schade offering potential solutions.

Arsenal's attacking depth could become increasingly important as they look to defend their Premier League crown and go one better in the Champions League.

The lack of natural options on the left side is a potential concern for Arteta, particularly with Christos Tzolis currently the only natural left-sided attacker available to him.

Although Arsenal have several players capable of filling wide positions, bringing in a specialist could provide greater balance and reduce the burden on their existing options.

The Norway international or the £22m Bees star would certainly offer Arteta another attacking dimension, while their ability to operate across the frontline would also provide valuable flexibility.

With ambitions of competing on multiple fronts, adding a left winger in January could give Arsenal a significant boost during the second half of the campaign.

#2275. Man Utd transfer news: Mauricio Pochettino puts prolific 19-goal Spurs target on January 'wishlist'

By Lewis Blain | 19 Nov 2026 07:21

Manchester United are preparing to strengthen their attack in the January transfer window, with new boss Mauricio Pochettino already keen to add more firepower.

The Argentine is understood to have put four main centre-forward targets on his winter 'wishlist', including a prolific striker also attracting interest from former club and top-flight rivals Tottenham Hotspur.

Benfica talisman Vangelis Pavlidis has emerged as a particularly exciting possibility after an outstanding start to the season in Portugal.

The Red Devils are ready to spend significantly to back Pochettino in January, with Benfica striker Pavlidis among the names under consideration, according to reports.

Spurs and Barcelona have also been linked with the Greece international, who is said to be open to a move to the Premier League in 2027.

One source revealed: "Pochettino is eager to make sure United have more of a goal threat. He's already been discussing names, and Vangelis Pavlidis is someone who fits the bill."

Pavlidis is not the only striker on United's radar, with Galatasaray's Victor Osimhen also admired by Pochettino. However, a deal for the Nigerian is considered less realistic due to his substantial transfer fee and wage demands.

Former Brentford forward Ivan Toney is another option, while Borussia Dortmund's Serhou Guirassy is being monitored as a potential rotation option, too.

Pavlidis has scored 19 goals in 19 appearances across all competitions this season, while also providing four assists. His remarkable return means he has found the net once every 72 minutes on average.

The 27-year-old's immense goalscoring numbers make him an attractive proposition for United, particularly with Benjamin Sesko having struggled with injuries.

His current form suggests he could provide an immediate improvement to Pochettino's attacking options, offering a reliable source of goals during the second half of the campaign.

Importantly, Pavlidis' age does not necessarily have to come at the expense of Sesko's long-term development, with the Slovenian still viewed as part of United's future once fully fit.

Osimhen may be the more ambitious acquisition, but Pavlidis appears to offer a potentially more attainable solution without sacrificing significant attacking quality.

If United are serious about challenging at the top end of the Premier League, adding a striker with 19 goals already this season could be a major step forward.

#2274. Chelsea transfer news: Blues learn new asking price for in-demand forward amid Man City interest

By Lewis Blain | 19 Nov 2026 07:39

Chelsea are facing growing competition for Club Brugge forward Nicolo Tresoldi, with Premier League rivals Manchester City among the clubs monitoring his situation.

The 23-year-old has attracted considerable attention after an impressive goalscoring campaign in Belgium, with several European sides now considering a move.

However, the asking price for the Germany-born striker has risen amid increasing interest, potentially complicating the Blues' pursuit.

Tresoldi has scored 14 goals in all competitions for Club Brugge this season, including five in the Champions League, with his hat-trick against Inter Milan a particular highlight.

North London duo Arsenal and Tottenham Hotspur have also been linked with the forward, while Serie A giants Juventus and AC Milan are closely monitoring his availability.

According to reports in Italy (via Sport Witness), Milan are currently considered the leading contenders, with Tresoldi's support for the Rossoneri potentially helping their pursuit.

However, Chelsea and City remain in the picture, although their chances of securing his signature could be complicated by Club Brugge's increasing demands.

Tresoldi was initially valued at more than €50m (£42m), but it is now believed that the Belgian outfit wants €60m (£50m) as they look to capitalise on the growing interest.

Milan are keen on a January move, with the player's preference for a switch to San Siro potentially giving them an advantage. Ultimately, though, their ability to meet Brugge's asking price could determine whether a deal is possible.

Although Tresoldi's performances make him an attractive target, Chelsea's chances of landing the forward appear rather complicated.

His preference for AC Milan is an obvious obstacle, particularly with the Italian club already keen to explore a January transfer.

There is also the question of where he would fit into Xabi Alonso's plans, with Joao Pedro currently Chelsea's first-choice centre-forward. City face a similar dilemma, given Erling Haaland's established position in their attack.

For Tresoldi, regular football should be an important factor in deciding his next move, particularly at this stage of his development.

Chelsea may therefore be better served exploring alternative forward options rather than committing significant resources to a player who could favour a move elsewhere and face competition for minutes at Stamford Bridge.

Tutto sommato soddisfatti gli altoatesini, con l’obiettivo salvezza serena che sembra alla portata al netto della mancanza dei 3 punti che si trascina da più di qualche giornata; tanto da rivedere per i lombardi, bene le 3 vittorie consecutive ma la strada verso i piani altissimi è ancora lunga e tortuosa. #2272. Bolton Wanderers team news and predicted XI vs Portsmouth with defensive reshuffle needed to improve leaky backline

Bolton Wanderers return from the international break looking to pick themselves up from a demoralising defeat to Derby County two weeks ago. Steven Schumacher’s team sit seven points above the bottom three but risk sliding at pace if they can’t begin to fix their defensive woes, having conceded 29 goals so far.

The biggest issue for the Trotters is their home form. BWFC have lost all but two of seven games at the Toughsheet Community Stadium, conceding 15 of their 29 goals at the ground. Wanderers have to find form at home, and will hope Saturday’s game can be the start.

Whilst Bolton Wanderers have been trending down, it is the opposite for Portsmouth. Despite losing 2-1 to Stoke City before the break, Pompey have won three of their last six, sitting just two points off the top eight.

John Mousinho’s side head to Bolton with a good record against their opponents and will be hopeful of returning to winning ways at 15:00 on Saturday.

Bolton Wanderers have welcomed back all four internationals without any injury concern.

Thierry Gale and Chris Forino, who faced each other whilst playing for Barbados and Saint Lucia, both came back unscathed. The same can be said for Gaizka Larrazabal and Josh Sheehan, who both played one game for their respective nations.

Eoin Toal and Gavin Bazunu could be available on Saturday after training this week.

Bazunu in particular could be a big boost for BWFC. Whilst Jack Bonham has proven to be a solid deputy, the Irishman has been an astute signing from Southampton, preventing 1.45 expected goals and claiming two clean sheets this season.

Elsewhere John McAtee, Ryan Hardie, Jayden Nelson and Ethan Erhahon all remain out injured.

Bonham; Stephenson, Forino, Davies, Famewo; Sheehan, Simons; Larrazabal, Rodrigues, Gale; Dalby

With Bazunu’s full status as of yet unconfirmed, Bonham keeps his place, but the defence is reshuffled. Akin Famewo moves to left-back in place of Max Conway, with Ben Davies taking his position in central defence.

Bolton Wanderers have need to stop conceding easy goals and they have performed better playing one central defender on either side of the back four. Famewo has done so before and played well. On Saturday, he will have the task of handling Adrian Segecic and Terry Devlin on the Pompey right.

Gaizka Larrazabal continues at right-wing having been one of Wanderers’ better players at Pride Park, whilst Gale also keeps his place, simply because he is one of the only wide men who often tracks back, something BWFC need to stop their defensive issues.

Bolton Wanderers’ clash with Portsmouth kicks off at 15:00 on Saturday afternoon.

#2270. NEC vs PSV Eindhoven - prediction, team news, lineups

By Adepoju Marvellous | 19 Nov 2026 08:04, Last updated: 19 Nov 2026 20:42

The 2026-27 Eredivisie season resumes with NEC welcoming PSV Eindhoven to Stadion de Goffert on Saturday evening, as both teams kick off matchday 13.

Both sides recorded impressive victories in their final outings before the November hiatus and will be aiming to build on that momentum.

With Feyenoord and Ajax dropping points against Heerenveen and FC Twente earlier in the day, PSV travelled to the Kooi Stadium to face Cambuur with the opportunity to move top of the Eredivisie table - a chance they seized emphatically with a 5-2 triumph.

That was a much-needed response from Peter Bosz’s men, who had suffered a 2-1 defeat to Club Brugge in the Champions League just four days earlier.

Playing before their title rivals gives De Rood-witten the chance to open up a four-point gap at the summit with a victory this weekend. However, away matches at NEC have been challenging recently, with the last four yielding just one win and 12 goals conceded.

PSV’s trip to Nijmegen is further complicated by a daunting assignment against Real Madrid next Tuesday, leaving Bosz with a delicate balancing act, especially after an international break in which several players were involved.

With a league-high six goals scored in the opening 15 minutes this season, Saturday’s visitors have an opportunity to exploit a notable weakness in NEC, who have conceded first in six of their last eight matches and in eight of the last 10 meetings between the sides.

After a difficult start to the season, NEC appear to be getting back on track, as a 3-2 victory over FC Utrecht on November 8 secured back-to-back wins for the first time since August and made three victories from their last five outings (D1, L1).

While confidence appears to be returning, Dick Schreuder’s side have yet to solve their defensive issues, conceding in 19 consecutive matches across all competitions; their last shutout came in the season opener against Olympiacos.

Having failed to keep a clean sheet in 20 consecutive meetings with PSV, the Eniesee know they will need to deliver in attack to stand any chance of success and have shown they can, scoring at least three goals in five of the last six encounters against this weekend’s opponents.

Saturday’s hosts also face a looming European assignment, giving Schreuder additional selection considerations as he seeks a third consecutive win over his opposite number in 2026, following two victories in March just 11 days apart.

For all of NEC’s recent renaissance, they are one of two Eredivisie teams - alongside Willem II - yet to win a league home match this season, so there is bound to be some level of apprehension among the supporters.

Emre Mor (groin) and Bram Nuytinck (ankle) have both been sidelined since September and remain unavailable for NEC.

Perr Schuurs is a doubt for this weekend after missing the win over Utrecht, while Dennis Geiger is still regaining full fitness.

Bryan Linssen has scored or assisted in each of his side’s last four meetings with PSV, and the veteran forward will be aiming to make an impact once again.

Ex-NEC player Sami Ouaissa will not feature against his former club due to a muscle problem that has hampered his debut campaign in Eindhoven.

Long-term absentees Adamo Nagalo, Jerdy Schouten and Alassane Plea are all still a long way from returning to action.

Meanwhile, Paul Wanner is set to make his comeback after the November international break, but Saturday’s clash is expected to come too soon for the Austrian.

Crettaz; Bras, Kaplan, Fonville; Kers, Nejasmic, Lebreton, Bakker; Chery, Tadic; Linssen

Kovar; Kostic, Obispo, Geertruida, Dest; Mauro Jr, Mijnans, Sano; Van Bommel, Til, Bajraktarevic

The last four meetings between PSV and NEC have produced a combined 24 goals, and all the evidence points to another entertaining encounter on Saturday.

PSV have shown vulnerability away from home at times, while NEC have yet to hit their stride in front of their own fans. Nevertheless, we expect PSV’s attacking threat to edge a high-scoring contest.

#2269. Lazio, Gattuso cambia solo la difesa: le scelte per il Lecce

Sarà la capolista ad aprire la dodicesima giornata di Serie A. La Lazio di Gennaro Gattuso ospita all'Olimpico il Lecce in quella che può avere il sapore della sfida trappola. In attesa di un mese e mezzo durissimo, che vedrà i biancocelesti giocare quasi solo big match, la partita di oggi non può e non deve essere sottovalutata. Lo ha spiegato bene Gattuso, facendo capire come la testa deve essere solo al Lecce. "La classifica si vede solo alle ultime 7-8 giornate" ha detto il tecnico calabrese, che però non vuole mollare quel primato e per farlo deve ottenere la decima vittoria in dodici giornate. Per la prima volta però la Lazio deve cambiare radicalmente la formazione vista l'assenza di tre titolarissimi come Sutalo, Tavares e Zaccagni (il quarto, Marusic, è stato sempre sostituito da Floriani Mussolini in questo inizio di stagione).

"Frattesi ha lavorato tutta la settimana con noi e sta bene" ha detto ieri Gattuso in conferenza stampa, lasciando così intendere che non ci saranno novità a centrocampo. Poteva avere un turno di riposo Belahyane, ma l'ex Verona dovrebbe partire ancora dal primo minuto insieme al centrocampista azzurro e Taylor. Davanti conferme per Isaksen a destra e Noslin a sinistra ai lati di Pinamonti, difeso ieri da Gattuso "ha fatto un primo tempo straordinario con il Napoli". Le novità saranno in difesa, con il solo Floriani Mussolini confermato rispetto alla formazione vista a Napoli. Al posto degli infortunati Sutalo e Tavares giocheranno i subentrati al Maradona, ovvero Diogo Leite e Pedraza. Al centro occasione anche per Doekhi, che parte in vantaggio su un Provstgaard pronto ad avere un turno di riposo dopo aver giocato già quasi 1500 minuti tra Lazio e nazionale in tre mesi di stagione.

#2264. Serie B, Catanzaro-Palermo: al Ceravolo una sfida agli antipodi

Alle 17:15 lo stadio "Nicola Ceravolo" ospita la sfida tra Catanzaro e Palermo, valevole per la dodicesima giornata del campionato di Serie B. L'incrocio mette di fronte gli estremi opposti della graduatoria: i calabresi occupano l'ultimo posto in solitaria con appena 6 punti, mentre i rosanero guidano la classifica da imbattuti a quota 27, a più quattro sul Modena secondo.

La gara arriva a sei mesi esatti dal confronto nella semifinale play-off dello scorso maggio, vinta all'andata in Calabria per 3-0 dai giallorossi prima del vano tentativo di rimonta siciliana al ritorno.

Nei precedenti in campionato senza considerare gli spareggi, il bilancio complessivo al "Ceravolo" premia i padroni di casa con 11 vittorie a 3 (nell'ultima stagione regolare terminò 1-0 con rete di Cissé, mentre l'ultimo successo esterno risale al 3-1 del 27 aprile 2025).

La direzione dell'incontro è affidata a Ermanno Feliciani.

COME ARRIVA IL CATANZARO - Situazione critica per la formazione allenata da Michele Mignani, fanalino di coda del torneo e chiamata a invertire immediatamente la rotta davanti al proprio pubblico per non perdere ulteriore terreno nella corsa salvezza.

Il tecnico giallorosso si affida al 3-5-2 e ritrova un elemento chiave in cabina di regia: rientra infatti Petriccione, assente per un problema fisico nell'ultimo turno pre-sosta a Cesena. Davanti a Pigliacelli, il terzetto difensivo sarà guidato da Antonini insieme a Frosinini e Imperiale, mentre sulle corsie esterne agiranno Candela e Dorval. A centrocampo, accanto a Petriccione, spazio a Mosti e uno tra i fratelli Tchaouna (Franck risulta tra gli indisponibili insieme a Loubao e Pafundi, mentre Franky è in lizza per una maglia). In attacco, accanto a Pecorino, tutti i riflettori sono puntati su capitan Iemmello, fermo a 2 gol in campionato ma già protagonista della doppietta che decise la sfida play-off di maggio.

COME ARRIVA IL PALERMO - Percorso netto finora per la squadra di Filippo Inzaghi, prima in classifica con 8 vittorie, 3 pareggi, 25 reti segnate e 13 subite. In trasferta i siciliani sono ancora imbattuti (3 successi e 3 pari, i più recenti sui campi di Pisa e Ascoli, con 14 gol fatti e 9 incassati).

In vista di un ciclo ravvicinato da tre gare in sette giorni, Inzaghi conferma il 4-2-3-1 fluido ma deve fare i conti con diverse assenze nel reparto arretrato: oltre al portiere Joronen, restano indisponibili Barba, Bani e Pierozzi, mentre Strefezza è attualmente fuori lista over e Palumbo resta in dubbio.

Tra i pali ci sarà Perin, protetto da Cassandro, Ceccaroni, Augello e uno tra Peda (favorito) e Magnani al centro. In mediana spazio a Ranocchia — a segno nell'ultimo turno ad Ascoli — affiancato da Mazzitelli, in vantaggio su Segre per l'ultima apparizione prima dell'avvicendamento in lista. Sulla trequarti confermati Hernani, Vavassori (6 centri, reduce da sindrome influenzale) e il capocannoniere Johnsen, già autore di 7 gol e 5 assist stagionali (oltre a 4 reti in carriera contro i calabresi), a supporto dell'unica punta Pohjanpalo (2 gol finora), rientrato dagli impegni con la Finlandia.

#2263. Newcastle United vs Arsenal - prediction, team news, lineups

By Ben Knapton | 19 Nov 2026 09:14, Last updated: 20 Nov 2026 18:06

A modern Premier League rivalry will be renewed on Saturday evening, when champions Arsenal make the long trek north to battle Newcastle United following the international break.

The Gunners entered the hiatus on the back of a fifth straight win, edging out Hull City 1-0, while Matthias Jaissle's inconsistent troops settled for a 1-1 draw at Fulham.

The Hull low block momentarily appeared capable of withstanding all of Arsenal's huffing and puffing two weekends ago, but the holders were belatedly rewarded for their endeavours when Martin Odegaard tucked home a second-half rebound.

Arsenal's task would have been easier had they been awarded a first-half penalty for Nobel Mendy's shirt pull on an offside Kai Havertz - the Premier League's Key Match Incidents panel has now ruled that the failure to award a spot kick was incorrect - but as champions do, they found a way to triumph while not at their finest.

Courtesy of their 34th 1-0 Premier League win under Mikel Arteta - the most by any coach since he took charge in December 2019 - and Manchester City's latest setback at Nottingham Forest, Arsenal opened up a five-point advantage at the top of the Premier League table before international football intervened.

Furthermore, the Gunners' total of 27 points from their opening 10 games of a Premier League season can only be bested by the 28 accrued by Chelsea in 2005-06, Manchester City in 2011-12, Man City again in 2017-18 and Liverpool in 2019-20; each of those teams went on to finish first.

While Arsenal's only loss of the top-flight campaign so far came away to Brighton & Hove Albion, they have won each of their other four road trips without conceding and have also kept four straight clean sheets altogether in the Premier League; not since January 2006 have they registered five straight shut-outs in the division.

Arsenal's four clean sheets in their last four Premier League games is more than Newcastle have managed across all competitions all season, Jaissle's men only keeping the opposition at bay three times and failing to do so at Craven Cottage on November 7.

A Jacob Murphy stunner preceded a slightly fortuitous Rodrigo Muniz equaliser for Fulham, extending Newcastle's winless run to three games in all tournaments and leaving them languishing in the bottom half.

Nevertheless, Jaissle labelled a point in London a "step in the right direction" after his side's harrowing home loss to Everton, as Murphy's fine left-footed strike continued the Magpies' sequence of scoring in every away game in 2026-27.

However, after scoring at least twice in each of their first four matches at St James' Park this season, Newcastle have now failed to net in each of their last two, going down 1-0 to Aston Villa and Everton in successive clashes.

The hosts were also subjected to an agonising one-goal loss in this fixture last term, as Gabriel Magalhaes completed a 2-1 Arsenal comeback win, ending the Gunners' three-match losing run in front of the St James' Park faithful.

Newcastle United form (all competitions):

Arsenal players did not drop like flies over the international break, contrary to some previous pauses, but the Gunners will be without Martin Zubimendi after the midfielder hurt his hamstring in Spain's 3-2 loss to England.

The second-string lynchpin joins Bukayo Saka (quad) and the suspended Max Dowman on the Arsenal sidelines, but Arteta will otherwise have an identical squad to the one that got the better of Hull.

Expect Bruno Guimaraes to appear from the off against his former employers, while Havertz should hold off the threat of Viktor Gyokeres despite the latter extending an all-time Nations League record in November.

On Newcastle's end, Jacob Ramsey returns from a one-match ban after his sending-off against Everton, while Nico Gonzalez and Joe Willock are also expected to be fine despite suffering from illness earlier in the week.

Joelinton's thigh injury will likely keep him out of this one, though, while backup goalkeeper Ewen Jaouen will be out for at least another few weeks with a hamstring problem of his own.

Ex-Arsenal target Matias Fernandez-Pardo has also returned from a troublesome illness of his own, and the 21-year-old may be given a shot over the underwhelming Yoane Wissa here.

Newcastle United possible starting lineup:

Hornicek; Dedic, Thiaw, Burn, Hall; Steur, Gonzalez; Murphy, Fernandez-Pardo, Barnes; Osula

Raya; Timber, Konsa, Gabriel, Calafiori; Guimaraes, Rice; Madueke, Odegaard, Tzolis; Havertz

While the break came at a better time for Newcastle than Arsenal, an increasingly familiar fate is set to befall the Magpies at St James' Park.

The champions' defensive might is showing no sign of wavering, nor is their ability to grind out victories in challenging circumstances, so we anticipate another 1-0 to the Arsenal.

#2262. Blackburn Rovers sweat over key duo’s availability against Burnley, but influential star is set to return

Blackburn Rovers face rivals Burnley on Sunday, and the big story leading up to the game has been Tony Mowbray’s withdrawal from head coach duties due to health issues. Damien Johnson is stepping into the role in the interim, and he and the first-team staff have a handful of injuries to deal with.

As relayed by local reporter Elliott Jackson, Johnson revealed that star striker Andri Gudjohnsen and midfield mainstay Sam Morsy are alongside Scott Wharton in requiring assessment before the game. All other players returning from international duty – Tom Atcheson, Augustus Kargbo and Kristi Montgomery – will be tested as well, Johnson has said.

One man whose fate has already been confirmed is Balazs Toth, after the goalkeeper broke a finger while away with Hungary. In better news though, Japanese playmaker Ryoya Morishita has trained over the break and is expected to be available.

Among those requiring assessment, there are some crucial Blackburn Rovers players.

Striker Gudjohnsen is perhaps the most important player in blue and white halves at the moment. The Icelandic talisman is in unassailable form, with 11 goals in 15 club games alongside four in two while away with his national side over the latest international break.

Morsy has become an influential player since his summer arrival. It perhaps wasn’t expected that he would become such a key starter, but his partnership in midfield with Montgomery has given this Rovers side some experience and bite. Ahead of a high-stakes game with aggression and tough tackles expected, having the Egyptian in the middle of the park will be key.

Toth is a big loss though, so it will be down to Aynsley Pears to step in. Sunday would have been a fitting game for Blackburn-born favourite Wharton to return, but there is understandable patience over his comeback.

Morishita’s return is a big boost, however. The 29-year-old is a key creative source, and even if he can only play from the bench, he can still make a telling impact.

Now, there’s no reason to doubt Johnson’s update, but sections of the Blackburn Rovers support will be hoping that there isn’t as much doubt surrounding the likes of Gudjohnsen and Morsy as has been let on.

Indecisive answers on their availability keep Burnley guessing. The rigorous preparation clubs go through before games nowadays means the Clarets will be prepared for all outcomes, but the uncertainty means they have to stay on their toes and plan carefully for all potential lineups.

One thing for sure is that all of Rovers’ doubts will be doing whatever they can to be fit for the fixture. Not only is this a great chance to end a 16-year wait for an East Lancashire derby win, but the players and staff will be determined to deliver a result for Mowbray after he stepped back earlier this week.

#2261. Tim Vickery shares Pep Guardiola Brazil update amid Carlo Ancelotti criticism

By Ben Knapton | 19 Nov 2026 09:18

Carlo Ancelotti is likely to remain Brazil head coach until at least the 2028 Copa America, regardless of Pep Guardiola's availability, according to South American football expert Tim Vickery.

The Italian is under growing pressure after a turbulent November window. He was sent off during Saturday's 3-2 friendly defeat to Japan, prompting Brazil great Romario to publicly demand his exit, before watching from the stands as his side thrashed Singapore 7-0 on Tuesday.

That followed a hugely disappointing World Cup, in which Brazil were knocked out in the last 16 by Norway, their earliest exit from the tournament since 1990.

Speaking exclusively to Sports Mole, Vickery admitted that Ancelotti's reign has fallen well short of expectations, but believes his contract, which runs until 2030, is keeping him in the job.

"If this was a run-of-the-mill Brazil coach, he could well already be receiving his marching orders, because there's no doubt about it: his time with Brazil has proved a massive disappointment so far," Vickery said.

"Through the World Cup it was acceptable and nothing more until, by his own confession, he made a very poor substitution against Norway and lost control of that game.

"Coming off an extremely disappointing World Cup, Brazilians want to win. They're not that patient, and they're not that interested in long-term tests.

"The key issue is one he hasn't solved: how do you reconcile a team with two wingers with the midfield resources Brazil have, and still remain compact and defensively solid?"

Guardiola has been out of work since leaving Manchester City at the end of 2025-26, and he has history with the Selecao. In late 2012, while on sabbatical in New York, he let it be known that coaching Brazil was the one job that could tempt him back, only for the CBF to rush through the appointment of Luiz Felipe Scolari.

Vickery believes Brazil would now be more open to Guardiola, but stressed that no change is imminent, with Ancelotti's next opportunity to impress not coming until the end of March.

"As a club coach, if things aren't going well, you try something on Wednesday and something else at the weekend. You can't do that as a national team coach," Vickery explained.

"The pressure is on him to come up with a coherent idea of a side, and if that means packing the midfield and giving up one of his wingers, that's the way he's going to have to go.

"The next international window is very important for him to win his credibility back, but I suspect at least until the Copa America of 2028, Carlo Ancelotti will be Brazil coach."

Guardiola has also been tipped to replace Mauricio Pochettino as USA manager following the latter's move to Manchester United, and Pochettino has publicly talked up the ex-Manchester City boss taking the job.

#2258. Burnley team news and predicted XI to face Blackburn Rovers as key player returns for derby day

Fabio Celestini has inherited a sinking ship with the monumental task of keeping Burnley afloat, with the international break arriving at the perfect time to put a temporary halt to their struggle.

The Clarets’ 5-4 capitulation at Cardiff City, where they were leading 4-1 by the 62nd minute, was a brutal reminder of where they find themselves. Celestini’s squad are devoid of confidence and leaking goals at will.

As it stands, Burnley sit 22nd in the Championship table with just one win in 15 games. They are already five points from safety and lurking in the bottom three alongside Derby County and Preston North End.

Blackburn Rovers meanwhile will feel they can finally end their 16-year wait for a victory over their arch rivals, buoyed by a 2-1 victory over promotion-chasing West Ham last time out.

Anel Ahmedhodzic could return to the fold having missed the trip to Cardiff City due to illness, but Conor Roberts will be absent after a red card in South Wales and will serve the first match of his three-game suspension.

Injuries have been an unfortunate feature of Dastan Satpayev’s time at Turf Moor since arriving on loan from Chelsea and he remains out with a recurring hamstring issue. Tunisian international Hannibal Mejbri will be absent with the same injury.

The defeat at Lincoln City brought an end to Nicky Hayen’s time in charge, and it also brought Amando Broja’s season to a temporary standstill with a groin injury. Fellow frontman Ashley Barnes remains out, but is nearing a return after featuring for the U21s side.

Jacob Bruun Larsen is out due to a stress fracture and Jordan Beyer remains a long-term absentee and is yet to make a competitive appearance since December 2023 due to a serious knee injury. Among those also unavailable are Ben Amos, Mike Tresor, Shurandy Sambo and Michael Obafemi.

In better news, Bashir Humphreys has been back in training and is on the brink of a return. The Blackburn Rovers clash may come too soon, but he could return next week.

Coudert; Walker, Ahmedhodzic, Ekdal, Pires; Cullen, Hatate; Ramazani, Edwards, Banel; Vardy.

Due to injuries, Celestini’s side pretty much picks for itself. However, there could be one key change in midfield.

Gregoire Coudert has only kept two clean sheets this term, but will line up between the sticks.

The full-back flanks will be occupied by Kyle Walker on the right and Lucas Pires on the left, while the returning Ahmedhodzic could partner Hjalmar Ekdal at centre-back in place of Igor Julio.

Josh Laurent and Reo Hatate have been the midfield pivot for the last two outings, but after three appearances off the bench, club captain and star player Josh Cullen could return to the starting lineup for the first time since his ACL injury in December 2025.

Marcus Edwards plays in attacking midfield, and with two goals in three games under Celestini, the right-wing position will likely fall to the in-form Largie Ramazani. Jayden Banel plays off the left.

The centre-forward spot is the one position which is up for grabs, with Andreas Hountondji beginning to show signs of promise. However, the experience of Jamie Vardy could prove crucial in a game of this significance.

Per Spalletti ci sono buone notizie, visto che Celik e Cambiaso hanno recuperato, mentre ieri Nico Gonzalez e Kalulu hanno ancora lavorato a parte. Il francese non ce la farà per domani e probabilmente tornerà a inizio dicembre. Per Nico Gonzalez resta qualche piccolissima speranza, anche se è più probabile rivederlo per la sfida contro l’Omonia Nicosia.

La Juventus ritrova due giocatori importanti in vista della gara contro il Venezia. Nella giornata di ieri, infatti, Celik e Cambiaso hanno ripreso ad allenarsi con il resto del gruppo, recuperando dai rispettivi problemi fisici. Una notizia sicuramente positiva per Luciano Spalletti, che rischiava di presentarsi alla sfida di domani in una situazione di forte emergenza. Il recupero di entrambi permette invece al tecnico bianconero di avere maggiori alternative, soprattutto sulle corsie esterne. Resta, però, da capire se i due giocatori saranno immediatamente impiegati dal primo minuto. Entrambi, infatti, arrivano da alcuni fastidi fisici e lo staff potrebbe preferire una gestione prudente delle loro condizioni. Non è quindi da escludere l’ipotesi di una staffetta nel corso della partita, con Spalletti che potrebbe alternarli per evitare di correre rischi inutili. Un’altra possibilità riguarda l’impiego di Cabal sulla corsia di sinistra, così da avere una soluzione alternativa e non sovraccaricare Cambiaso. Le prossime ore saranno importanti per capire quali saranno le scelte definitive dell’allenatore. In ogni caso, poter contare nuovamente su Celik e Cambiaso rappresenta un’ottima notizia per la Juventus, soprattutto considerando i numerosi impegni ravvicinati.

Tra gli investimenti più importanti della Juventus c’è sicuramente Kerim Alajbegovic. I bianconeri sono stati abili a strapparlo al Chelsea, anticipando la concorrenza e assicurandosi uno dei profili più interessanti in prospettiva. Il bosniaco, in poco tempo, è riuscito a ritagliarsi uno spazio importante all’interno della squadra, dimostrando di avere qualità tecniche e personalità nonostante la giovane età. Le sue prestazioni hanno confermato le aspettative della società, anche se è evidente che ci siano ancora margini di crescita. La Juventus crede molto nelle potenzialità del giocatore e la volontà è quella di accompagnarne il percorso di maturazione, rendendolo sempre più centrale nel progetto bianconero. L’obiettivo, infatti, è costruire anche attorno a lui il futuro della squadra, valorizzando un investimento sul quale la dirigenza ha deciso di puntare con convinzione. Per questo motivo, eventuali offerte che potrebbero arrivare nei prossimi mesi, soprattutto durante la finestra di mercato di gennaio, difficilmente verrebbero prese in considerazione. La posizione della Juventus appare piuttosto chiara: Alajbegovic non è un giocatore sul quale fare valutazioni in ottica di cessione, ma una risorsa da valorizzare. A rendere ancora più semplice questa prospettiva c’è anche la volontà dello stesso calciatore, che a Torino sta molto bene. Le premesse per proseguire insieme, dunque, sembrano esserci tutte.

By Carter White | 19 Nov 2026 11:15

Aiming to further strengthen their survival bid, Notts County welcome playoff-chasing Stevenage to Meadow Lane for an all-important League One clash on Saturday afternoon.

The Magpies enjoyed a promising unbeaten run prior to the November international break, whilst the Boro also experienced success in both the FA Cup and EFL Trophy.

For the first time since the 2014-15 campaign, Notts County are back in the third tier of English football, with the East Midlanders aiming to avoid a season of bottom-four struggle in League One.

Since a 2-0 defeat by Wycombe Wanderers on October 17, the Magpies have strung together a five-game unbeaten run (W4 D1) across third tier, FA Cup and EFL Trophy action, including a 3-0 away success at Burton Albion in the latter competition on November 10.

After collecting a commendable tally of seven points from their past three third-tier contests, Notts County have moved up to 18th position in the League One standings, two points above the relegation spaces.

A worrying statistic ahead of Saturday afternoon, only Peterborough United (5), Barnsley (5) and whipping boys Bromley (5) have earned fewer than the Magpies' eight home points in the division so far.

Head coach Martin Paterson was heralded as a hero after guiding Notts County to League Two playoff victory during this first campaign in charge, however, the former Tampa Bay Rowdies assistant now faces a different type of task at a higher level of the English pyramid.

Just days before the announcement of his contract extension, Stevenage captain Carl Piergianni netted the only goal of the Kassam Stadium contest to knock Oxford United out of the FA Cup at the first-round stage.

At the beginning of the following week, the Boro enjoyed an EFL Trophy win over Fulham Under-21s, meaning that Alex Revell's troops were three games unbeaten (W2 D1) across all competitions heading into the November pause.

Stevenage were losing semi-finalists to Stockport County in last season's playoffs, and the Hertfordshire outfit are currently way off the top-six pace, sitting seven points behind sixth-placed Plymouth Argyle.

Following Jamie Reid's summer switch to divisional rivals Reading, the Boro have shared the goalscoring responsibilities amongst their ranks, with Dan Kemp, Josh Magennis and Oliver Sanderson all netting on three occasions in League One so far.

Due to Notts County's recent plights in non-league and League Two, Saturday's meeting at Meadow Lane will be the first between the two sides since March 2019, with the Magpies unbeaten in their past four battles (W2 D2) against Stevenage dating back to November 2017.

Both returning from international duty with Republic of Ireland Under-21s, Darius Lipsiuc and Rory Finneran could feature this weekend for Notts County.

Back from two substitute appearances for Sierra Leone in Africa Cup of Nations qualifying, Charlton Athletic loanee Daniel Kanu is looking for his seventh League One start of the term.

Nicholas Tsaroulla has spent time away on Nations League duty for Cyprus and could be an option in the middle of the park or at wing-back for the Magpies at Meadow Lane.

Scoring for Stevenage in the most recent league match at Leicester City on October 31, Ben Broggio and Tugra Turhan are pushing for starts.

There is still plenty of experience within the Boro ranks, though, with Northern Irishman Magennis and midfielder Louis Thompson expected to make the XI.

#2253. Real Madrid vs. Celta Vigo: Head-to-head record and past meetings ahead of La Liga clash

By Matt Law | 19 Nov 2026 11:20, Last updated: 19 Nov 2026 11:20

Real Madrid will be aiming to continue their excellent run of form when they resume their La Liga campaign against Celta Vigo on Saturday night.

Los Blancos have been victorious in each of their last eight matches in all competitions, including a 3-0 success over Valencia before the November international break.

Jose Mourinho's side are currently second in the La Liga table, three points behind the leaders Barcelona, boasting a record of 10 wins and two draws from their 12 league matches during the 2026-27 campaign.

Celta, meanwhile, have a record of two wins, seven draws and three defeats from their 12 games during the current season.

The Vigo outfit are currently 11th in the division on 13 points.

Here, Sports Mole looks at the head-to-head record and past meetings between Real Madrid and Celta ahead of their clash in the Spanish capital this weekend.

These two sides have locked horns on 139 occasions throughout history, and Real Madrid comfortably lead the head-to-head record, having won 83 times to Celta's 35, while there have also been 21 draws.

Real Madrid have netted 324 times against Celta, conceding 175, while Cristiano Ronaldo is the all-time leading goalscorer in this fixture, having scored 20 goals against the Sky Blues during his time with Los Blancos.

Unsurprisingly, Real Madrid have also dominated their La Liga meetings, winning 73 of their 120 matches, suffering 30 defeats in the process, while there have been 17 draws.

Notably, three of Celta's last four wins over Real Madrid have actually come at the Bernabeu, while they have only beaten Los Blancos once in front of their own supporters since May 2001.

Real enjoyed a 10-game winning run against Celta in La Liga between May 2014 and December 2025, when Os Celestes emerged 2-0 winners in the Spanish capital during their most recent clash at Bernabeu.

Xabi Alonso's side came into that game desperate to keep up with Barcelona in the title race, but after conceding the first goal to Williot Swedberg's brilliant flick early in the second half, frustrations grew.

Madrid left-back Fran Garcia picked up two yellow cards in around sixty seconds shortly beyond the hour mark, before Alvaro Fernandez Carreras saw a straight red for abusing the referee in stoppage time.

To make matters worse, Swedberg then added a second when he was played through by Celta legend Iago Aspas and dribbled around Thibaut Courtois before walking the ball into an empty net.

However, Real Madrid were able to gain some revenge in the reverse meeting in Vigo in March 2026, with the capital giants recording a last-gasp 2-1 in.

Celta will now be aiming to make it back-to-back wins over Real Madrid at Bernabeu.

#2251. Manchester City vs Fulham - prediction, team news, lineups

By Oliver Thomas | 19 Nov 2026 11:25, Last updated: 20 Nov 2026 21:18

Seeking to end a three-game losing run in the Premier League, Manchester City welcome Fulham to the Etihad Stadium for Saturday’s lunchtime kickoff.

These two teams last butted heads nine months ago when three first-half goals helped the Citizens claim a comfortable 3-0 home victory.

Man City are just the second team in English top-flight history – after Sheffield Wednesday back in 1904-05 – to win their first seven matches of a season and then lose their next three, suffering defeat to Aston Villa, Brighton & Hove Albion and Nottingham Forest before the international break.

Liam Delap haunted his former club by scoring twice in a deserved 3-1 home victory for Forest a fortnight ago, with costly defensive mistakes and a lack of intensity proving to be key factors behind the downfall of Enzo Maresca’s side.

Only Sunderland (six) and Hull City (five) have committed more errors leading to goals in the top flight this season than Man City (four), who have slipped to third in the Premier League table and six points off the summit ahead of a crunch trip to leaders Arsenal at the Emirates at the end of this month.

The international break has given City a welcome chance to reset, and Maresca has insisted there is no reason to panic just yet, even though his side have shipped 17 goals across their last eight games in all competitions, including eight first-half goals in 10 league outings.

After losing 1-0 to Brighton in their most recent home fixture, Man City are in danger of suffering back-to-back league defeats at the Etihad for the first time since May 2021, while they have not lost successive home games without scoring since December 2008.

However, Man City should be confident of success on Saturday, as they have won each of their last 20 meetings with Fulham across all competitions, including 17 straight victories in the Premier League – the longest winning run against the same opponent in English football history.

Fulham extended their unbeaten run in the Premier League to four matches (W2 D2) after coming from behind to rescue a point in a 1-1 draw with Newcastle United at Craven Cottage before the international break.

Jacob Murphy thought he had scored the winner with a superb curling strike 10 minutes from time, but Fulham restored parity just three minutes later when substitute Rodrigo Muniz stabbed home an equaliser – his first Premier League goal since the opening weekend of last season.

Head coach Alvaro Arbeloa was pleased with his side’s reaction to going a goal down late in the tight contest, and Fulham now sit 15th in the table, two points above the relegation zone and three points behind the top half.

Fulham rank third for both accurate passes per game (493.4) and touches in the opposition’s box (327), and fifth for average possession (57.7%) in the Premier League this season, yet their dominance on the ball has failed to translate into goals, with only Sunderland (eight) and Nottingham Forest (nine) finding the net fewer times than the Cottagers (10) after 10 matches.

Tricky tests against Man City, Bournemouth and Tottenham are up next for Fulham, who head into Saturday’s fixture seeking to win their first match at the Etihad since April 2009 when Clint Dempsey netted a brace in a 3-1 victory for Roy Hodgson’s side.

Man City attacker Antoine Semenyo remains sidelined with a knee injury, while Matheus Nunes (unspecified) and Abdukodir Khusanov (knock) are both "doubtful" according to Maresca.

However, Marc Guehi has taken part in training this week and should be ready to play after withdrawing from the England squad with a hip problem.

If Maresca decides against starting Guehi, this could open the door for 20-year-old Brazil international Vitor Reis to start his first PL match under the new City boss alongside captain Ruben Dias, while Josko Gvardiol is fit and ready to rival Nico O'Reilly for the left-back berth after sustaining an unspecified injury against Forest.

Maresca has a big decision to make over whether to continue with Enzo Fernandez alongside star midfielder Elliot Anderson or recall Ayyoub Bouaddi to play in a double pivot. Rayan Cherki, meanwhile, will likely retain his place in the number 10 role.

One player certain to start is Erling Haaland, who has scored eight goals in as many games against Fulham, while he has scored 20 goals in as many matches for club and country so far this season, netting three times in two games for Norway during the international break.

As for Fulham, Tom Cairney is available for selection after recovering from a niggling knee issue, but Emile Smith Rowe (hip) and Benjamin Lecomte (Achilles) are both ruled out through injury.

Man United-linked Calvin Bassey will likely start alongside David Affengruber at centre-back, while Man City academy graduates Shea Charles and Oscar Bobb are both expected to play in midfield and on the right wing respectively.

After scoring as a substitute last time out, striker Rodrigo Muniz will be pushing to start his first PL game of the season ahead of Gonzalo Garcia, who is without a goal in each of his last nine starts since netting on the opening weekend of the season.

Manchester City possible starting lineup:

Donnarumma; Lewis, Dias, Guehi, Gvardiol; Anderson, Bouaddi; Ndiaye, Cherki, Doku; Haaland

Leno; Castagne, Affengruber, Bassey, Robinson; Berge, Charles; Bobb, King, Iwobi; Garcia

Maresca will demand a strong reaction from his Man City players following recent setbacks, and their exceptional historical record in this fixture firmly establishes them as the favourites to claim all three points.

While a porous Citizens backline offers encouragement to a Fulham outfit that has drawn a blank in seven of their last 12 Premier League games on the road, we expect the hosts' attacking firepower to comfortably outgun the Cottagers on this occasion.

#2250. Dundee vs Hearts - prediction, team news, lineups

By Calum Burrowes | 19 Nov 2026 11:25, Last updated: 19 Nov 2026 11:33

Without a win in any competition for almost a month, under-pressure Wouter Vrancken takes his Hearts side to Dens Park this weekend to face Dundee as the Scottish Premiership returns following the international break.

The Dee headed into the international break in fifth place after a narrow 2-1 defeat to St Johnstone, while the Jambos suffered a third consecutive loss after being thrashed 5-2 at home by an in-form Falkirk side.

After a relatively quiet end to the 2025-26 season, during which Dundee neither threatened relegation nor came close to securing European football, Steven Pressley entered the new campaign hoping to take his side a step further.

Now approaching his 18th month in charge at Dens Park, Pressley has overseen five wins, two draws and five defeats in the opening 12 matches of the 2026-27 season, leaving the Dee fifth in the table and with an opportunity to leapfrog their upcoming opponents with a victory on Saturday afternoon.

Dundee won just one of their four matches leading up to the international break, a comfortable victory over bottom side Kilmarnock, while a draw against Rangers and 2-1 defeats to St Mirren and St Johnstone suggest the two-week stoppage may have come at a welcome time for Pressley's side.

A key factor behind their early-season fortunes has been their solid defensive record, with the side based on the north bank of the Firth of Tay conceding just 15 goals in their opening 12 Scottish Premiership outings, making their latest defeat all the more frustrating.

Dundee suffered an error-strewn defeat in the Tayside derby in Perth at the start of the month, losing 2-1 to St Johnstone, and will know that individual mistakes have prevented them from climbing further up the table.

Despite their inconsistent form, the Dee remain just four points behind third-placed Falkirk, with a game in hand, and will know that a victory against a depleted Hearts side this weekend could further strengthen their impressive start to the campaign.

As for the visitors, Hearts make the journey north from the Scottish capital on the back of three consecutive defeats across the Scottish League Cup, Conference League and Scottish Premiership.

Their cup run came to an end against rivals Hibernian before a 3-1 defeat to Swiss side Thun left them without a European victory all season, with a 5-2 league loss to high-flying Falkirk just days later only increasing the pressure on Vrancken.

An injury-hit Hearts side entered their latest Scottish Premiership fixture hoping to extend their 26-match unbeaten run at Tynecastle Park, but were left stunned after falling 4-0 behind at half-time before eventually losing 5-2, allowing John McGlynn's side to replace the Jambos in third place.

The humiliating defeat, which the Belgian boss described as "embarrassing" and a "disgrace", leaves Hearts 11 points behind league leaders Celtic, having held a seven-point advantage this time last year, and entering the final international break of the year knowing they need a significant points haul to get their campaign back on track.

A demanding 39-day period in which Hearts will play 11 matches before the end of the calendar year leaves precious little time for recovery, with their challenging schedule including encounters against Celtic and Rangers, another Edinburgh derby and three further Conference League fixtures in a league phase where their hopes of qualification are fading.

One positive for the Jambos is that they have won their last four meetings with Dundee, most recently coming from behind to secure a 2-1 victory in September thanks to two late goals, the second of which arrived in the 96th minute.

Dundee entered the international break without any further injury concerns following their defeat to St Johnstone and could therefore name an unchanged starting XI, although Pressley may be tempted to make alterations after some of his half-time substitutes impressed.

Joe Westley made an immediate impact after coming on at half-time, scoring less than three minutes later, and could now earn a starting place at the expense of either Ashley Hay or Bradley Fink.

Bobby Wales has not featured for the Dee in more than two months but is gradually working his way back to fitness and could make the matchday squad this weekend, although he will not be joined by Finlay Robertson or Simon Murray, who are both expected to remain sidelined until August 2027.

Meanwhile, Hearts' recent struggles could be partly put down to their ongoing injury crisis.

The Jambos are once again expected to be without as many as 11 players, with the Edinburgh-based side hoping to avoid any further setbacks before the January transfer window.

Players such as Stephen Kingsley, Tomas Magnusson and Blair Spittal are expected to return in the coming weeks, while Harry Milne and Calvin Miller are likely to remain sidelined for several months.

Preston North End have had a disastrous season so far which has left them rock bottom of the Championship after 15 games. PNE are already seven points adrift of safety, with a managerial change to Graham Alexander so far not working out.

North End currently have four loanees in their squad; Caleb Wiley, Liam Gibbs, Delano Burgzorg and Jusef Erabi. PNE have already brought in Greg Docherty under Alexander but he is clearly far from done with his Preston squad overhaul.

#2248. Il sorriso di Tikus, la forza di Thuram. E l'Inter vuole tenerselo stretto

Lo chiamano Tikus da quando era bambino. Un soprannome nato quasi per gioco, dall'unione di due parole: la parte finale di petit, piccolo in francese, e quella di Marcus. Un nomignolo che gli è rimasto addosso anche quando i centimetri sono diventati 192 e il fisico quello di un attaccante capace di spostare i difensori. Tikus, però, è rimasto Tikus. E forse è proprio in questo contrasto che si nasconde una parte del fascino di Marcus Thuram. Un gigante che gioca con il sorriso, che ama scherzare e far ridere gli altri, ma che all'Inter ha imparato a prendersi le responsabilità da protagonista. L'intesa con il capitano Lautaro Martinez è diventata uno degli elementi più riconoscibili della squadra, una coppia capace di completarsi attraverso caratteristiche differenti. Ma ridurre Thuram al ruolo di partner dell'argentino sarebbe ormai limitante. Marcus ha dimostrato di poter essere un riferimento offensivo anche quando il Toro non c'è. Lo sta facendo anche in questa stagione, nella quale l'assenza di Lautaro gli ha consegnato una responsabilità ulteriore che ha affrontato continuando a essere presente e incisivo.

La fotografia statistica fin qui racconta perfettamente il suo rendimento. In Serie A Thuram ha collezionato dieci presenze, otto da titolare, per complessivi 723 minuti. Sei gol e due assist, per un totale di otto partecipazioni dirette alle reti nerazzurre. In Champions League ha aggiunto quattro presenze e 274 minuti, senza ancora trovare la via del gol o dell'assist. Il bilancio complessivo nelle due competizioni è dunque di 14 apparizioni, 997 minuti, sei reti e due assist. In campionato è il miglior marcatore dell'Inter, un dato che rende l'idea della sua importanza nella produzione offensiva della squadra. La sua caratteristica più evidente è la capacità di mettere insieme qualità apparentemente distanti: la progressione palla al piede, la forza nel corpo a corpo, la disponibilità a lavorare per la squadra e la sensibilità nel dialogare con chi gli gioca accanto. Thuram non è un centravanti statico che aspetta il pallone in area. Gli piace partire, muoversi lateralmente, venire incontro e poi attaccare nuovamente lo spazio. È proprio questa mobilità a renderlo un elemento prezioso per il gioco nerazzurro. Quando può puntare l'avversario in velocità, sfruttando la falcata e la struttura fisica, diventa difficile da contenere. E anche quando non trova la conclusione può creare le condizioni per il gol di un compagno.

Il presente di Thuram si intreccia inevitabilmente con il futuro. Il suo contratto con l'Inter scade nel giugno 2028, ma il club ha già avviato il percorso per provare a prolungare l'accordo fino al 2031. Un segnale della volontà nerazzurra di continuare a puntare sul francese, ma anche una trattativa che si presenta tutt'altro che semplice. Marcus percepisce attualmente circa sei milioni di euro netti a stagione e punta a un rialzo. Sul tavolo c'è anche il tema della clausola rescissoria da 85 milioni di euro, un elemento che potrebbe diventare parte integrante della discussione sul nuovo accordo. L'obiettivo dell'Inter resta quello di allungare il rapporto fino al 2031, ma per arrivarci servirà trovare un punto d'incontro sulle condizioni economiche e sulle garanzie contrattuali. Il sorriso resta, la leggerezza pure. Ma dietro quell'atteggiamento c'è un calciatore che ha imparato a convivere con responsabilità sempre più grandi. Tikus non ha bisogno di cambiare espressione per diventare un protagonista. Gli basta il pallone tra i piedi, uno spazio da attaccare e quella falcata che a San Siro ormai conoscono bene. L'Inter vuole continuare a costruire il proprio futuro anche intorno a lui. Adesso la partita si sposta anche sul tavolo delle trattative. Trovare un accordo significherebbe dare continuità a un rapporto che, sul campo, ha già prodotto risultati importanti.
