#2246. Bolton Wanderers dodged a transfer bullet in walking away from £3m striker pursuit amid new blow: Opinion

On Deadline Day, both Bolton Wanderers and Preston North End were in the market for a striker. At the last hour, Celtic striker Johnny Kenny, who had spent the second half of the season prior at the Trotters, was made available.

Steven Schumacher‘s side would make a move for the 23-year-old, but Celtic’s price tag and PNE’s £3m offer would see BWFC walk away from a deal. And, Republic of Ireland international Kenny duly made the move to Deepdale.

Now though, according to The Lancashire Post’s Dan Murphy, Kenny is now out for the next couple of months with a hamstring injury. The striker’s injury struggles have meant that, in hindsight, Bolton Wanderers were better off looking elsewhere.

At the start of summer, Schumacher made it clear he’d welcome back a number of Bolton Wanderers loan stars, including Johnny Kenny. However, BWFC would not have the same spending power they had in League One, and being frugal with their budget would be at the top of the priority list.

The Trotters made 13 signings and Kenny would have been their marquee addition of the summer. The 23-year-old scored six goals, with a total of nine goal involvements in his time with the Trotters. In the end, the £3m price tag put Wanderers off, and they look right to have walked away.

Kenny has two goals so far for Preston North End but has played just eight games, having also suffered an injury that made him miss the play-offs with BWFC. The North West outfit would not have been able to bring in as many players if they had signed the forward, and so far they are nine points above their Lancashire rivals.

So far, Kenny has missed three games for the Lilywhites and could now miss at least the next 15 as he recovers from his new hamstring injury. The striker was one of Preston North End’s big signings, and with both them and Bolton Wanderers in a relegation fight, it is easy to see which team is in a better position after their summer recruitment.

For the Trotters, £3m is a lot of money, if not all their transfer budget, and it looks like they’ve dodged a serious problem by walking away from the deal.

Theo Bair, who signed the same day as Kenny joined PNE, has three goal involvements whilst Sam Dalby has six goals, sitting sixth in the Championship goalscoring charts. BWFC may still need more firepower at the top of the pitch, but they also need players on the pitch, and right now their double act is doing just that, whilst Kenny remains sidelined.

#2244. Barcelona transfer news: Hansi Flick wants new full-back as well as centre-back

By Axel Clody | 19 Nov 2026 11:43

Barcelona want to prioritise signing a centre-back in the upcoming winter transfer window, but Hansi Flick also wants a new full-back.

Barcelona have won the last two La Liga titles, but have suffered two Champions League failures, in the semi-final against Inter Milan and in the quarter-final against Atletico Madrid.

The defence has always been the main reason for Flick’s side falling short. This has been confirmed again this season, with the heavy defeat away at Paris Saint-Germain (4-2) and the loss to Real Madrid (1-2).

After choosing not to reinforce the defensive axis this summer, Barcelona intend to address the problem in 2027. Flick wants to strengthen his defence with the signings of a centre-back and a full-back, according to Marca.

The Catalan club’s priority is to sign a centre-back. The La Liga champions are working hard and exploring multiple options in that area.

While Josko Gvardiol and Jonathan Tah are options to forget about, Lisandro Martinez has everything of an interesting option, with his contract at Manchester United expiring at the end of the season.

Indeed, Barcelona will have limited resources in the winter transfer window, unless Jules Kounde and Alejandro Balde, who will not be held back if satisfactory offers arrive, leave. Faced with the disappointing performances of the Frenchman and the Spaniard, Barcelona are therefore also in search of a new full-back.

Joao Cancelo (32) is not the club’s future, while the two Masia graduates, Xavi Espart and Brian Farinas, are being used as full-backs but are midfielders by training.

‘Therefore, the arrival of a full-back is urgent and, barring a surprise, it will not happen in January. The aim is to sign one this summer for the following season’ - Marca noted.

Flick will therefore probably have to make do with his full-backs until the end of the season, before a planned arrival in the next summer transfer window. The Spanish outlet mentions right-back Pedro Porro as a way of strengthening that area, but the Spanish world champion is under contract with Tottenham Hotspur until June 2031.

#2243. Vitor Matos admits ‘anger’ expectation after leaving Swansea City to join Strasbourg

Swansea City confirmed on Wednesday that head coach Vitor Matos had left the club to take charge of Strasbourg, with the Portuguese manager’s appointment officially announced. The Swans received compensation of around £5m, with Leon Britton and Kris O’Leary taking charge on an interim basis, alongside former defender Ashley Williams.

Matos’ departure came as a shock and it has left sour feelings from Swansea City fans. Speaking ahead of his first game as Strasbourg boss, Matos has revealed his feelings towards his former side, and what he expects from the Swansea fans.

As reported by French news outlet ICI Alsace, Matos said: “It was a difficult decision to leave Swansea. I expect nothing from the fans but anger, and I understand it“.

He added:“It’s a really exciting project and I felt I could help move the team forward. It wasn’t an easy decision to leave Swansea, given the emotional dimension and emotional bonds I had forged there.”

It does not seem a replacement for Matos is close with Britton, O’Leary and Williams in charge for the clash against West Brom. Rob Edwards and Tommy Elphick are reportedly on the clubs radar as potentially replacements.

Swansea City will be frustrated to lose another manager just as they appeared to be building towards something special. The situation is reminiscent of Russell Martin’s departure, when he was poached by Southampton.

The Swans made significant progress under Matos, so finding a successor who can maintain a similar tactical approach should be a priority. Doing so would allow them to get the best out of the existing squad while limiting the disruption and adjustment period that inevitably comes with an unexpected change in the dugout.

Swansea City will be carefully weighing up a number of candidates, but there will be an urgency to appoint Matos’ replacement as quickly and efficiently as possible. The longer the vacancy remains, the greater the risk of disrupting the momentum they had begun to build.

Swansea City took a bold gamble when appointing Matos last season. He arrived with very little experience as a head coach, but the decision has proved to be an inspired one and could earn the club a substantial compensation fee at the very least.

With his departure looking increasingly likely amid his interest in the Strasbourg job, the Swans should have plenty of confidence in their ability to find a suitable replacement. The Matos appointment itself is evidence of their judgement, while their recent transfer windows have also been particularly impressive.

Overall, Swansea’s recruitment of both players and staff has been very strong and has helped transform them into a much more competitive side. There is little reason to doubt that their next managerial appointment could be another success.

#2241. Chelsea transfer news: The three positions where the Blues need January reinforcements

By Axel Clody | 19 Nov 2026 11:54, Last updated: 19 Nov 2026 11:54

Chelsea’s start to the season under Xabi Alonso has been marked by a contradiction that is hard to ignore: while the attack is posting impressive numbers and places the team among the most productive in the Premier League, the defensive system is far from offering the same security.

Chelsea score goals regularly, but also struggle to stop opponents from hitting the net. The result is an unbalanced team that is still looking for an identity capable of sustaining its ambitions in the competition.

After ten games, Chelsea sit fifth with 16 points, from five wins, one draw and four defeats. They have scored 19 goals, the third-best attack in the league, but conceded 16, the seventh-worst defence. The difference between the two areas helps explain why the team still cannot turn its attacking potential into a more consistent campaign.

There is no clear indication yet on whether the club will return to the market in January. The board were very active in the last window, investing more than £340m (€400m) on signings while raising an even larger sum from player sales. The financial balance of the movements was positive, but that does not mean the squad is complete.

Chelsea still have important gaps, some of them highlighted precisely by the difficulties of the first few months of the season. If they decide to make use of the winter window, the London club do not necessarily need another overhaul. Three positions, however, deserve special attention and could receive targeted reinforcements capable of raising the team’s competitive level.

The defence has been Chelsea’s main problem. More than the number of goals conceded, it is the way they happen that is worrying: individual errors, positioning difficulties and a disorganisation that repeats from game to game. The unit lacks a player capable of giving his team-mates security and offering consistency in a team that often allows opponents dangerous situations.

Wesley Fofana has accumulated errors, while Levi Colwill is going through a very negative start to the season. Maxence Lacroix, signed in the last window, has also yet to convince and justify the expectation placed on his arrival. The picture makes it clear that Alonso needs more reliable alternatives to organise the defensive line.

In that context, signing an experienced centre-back, capable of playing in different positions in defence, would be especially interesting. It is not just a matter of adding another name to the squad, but of finding someone who can take responsibility, guide his team-mates and offer tactical solutions to the manager.

Jules Kounde is an example of a profile that could meet that need. The Frenchman has been linked with Chelsea in recent days and faces an uncertain future at Barcelona. Alongside his experience at the highest level, his versatility to play both in the centre and on the right of defence would be an advantage. In a squad that needs more stability, a player of this profile would be an important addition.

Midfield also deserves attention, especially given the way Chelsea ended the last transfer window. The departure of Enzo Fernandez to Manchester City, in a deal that brought in more than £119m (€140m), left a considerable gap on the very last day of the window.

The London club ended a financially busy window without finding a worthy replacement, or at least someone capable of filling part of the Argentine’s importance.

The attempt to sign Lamine Camara was never completed. Chelsea came very close to agreeing a deal in the final hours of the window, but negotiations with Monaco collapsed. As a result, Moises Caicedo remained the main reference in the area, carrying even greater responsibility in building and sustaining the midfield.

The problem is that the Ecuadorian has had physical issues at the start of the season and is not always available. When he plays, he shows the quality to protect the defence and balance the team. The question is finding someone who can share those duties with him.

Valentin Barco appears as an interesting alternative because of his talent, but there is still a gap between recognising his potential and handing him such a big responsibility in the Premier League. The Argentine has not been used much, which also makes it difficult to judge whether he is ready for a bigger role.

Romeo Lavia, for his part, has been a pleasant surprise. The Belgian has the quality to perform the role and has managed a run of games, which is important given the physical problems that marked his last seasons. Even so, his injury history prevents Chelsea from treating his availability as a certainty.

Jordan Henderson offers experience and has appeared as another alternative, but at 36 it would not be reasonable to expect the Englishman to permanently solve the area’s shortcomings.

A good-level midfielder, with the physical capacity and quality to play alongside Caicedo, would give the manager more options and prevent the team’s performance from depending so much on the Ecuadorian’s presence.

The third need is at the head of the attack. Joao Pedro is an essential piece for Alonso, but Chelsea need a more reliable alternative for the moments when the Brazilian is unavailable. The striker’s recent injury again exposed an issue that was already showing in the manager’s choices: the difficulty of finding another player capable of taking on the role with similar confidence.

Danny Welbeck was signed precisely to offer that option. The Englishman did score in the Carabao Cup, but has received few opportunities in the Premier League. Even with Joao Pedro recently sidelined by injury, Alonso turned little to the experienced centre-forward, which raises doubts about how much he is really in plans for more frequent involvement.

It is possible to question whether age weighs in this assessment or whether Welbeck’s style does not exactly match what the Spanish manager is looking for in the position. Whatever the explanation, the fact is that the veteran has yet to establish himself as a reliable alternative to the Brazilian.

Emanuel Emegha also does not appear, at least at the moment, to be the solution Chelsea are looking for. The squad lacks a penalty-box centre-forward who can offer physical presence, attack the last line and turn the chances created by the team into goals. Someone who does not necessarily have to compete with Joao Pedro for a starting place, but who is able to replace him without a significant drop in performance.

#2240. West Ham United vs Preston North End - prediction, team news, lineups

By Matthew Cooper | 19 Nov 2026 11:54

West Ham United will be looking to pick up an important victory when they welcome Preston North End to London Stadium on Saturday.

The hosts currently sit ninth in the Championship table, while the visitors are rock bottom.

West Ham return to the London Stadium for the first time since Scott Parker's appointment as head coach, with the former midfielder looking to pick up his first victory.

The Hammers are without a win in their last four league games and have picked up just one point under Parker so far, producing a dramatic comeback to draw 3-3 with Cardiff City in his first game.

However, that was followed by a disappointing 2-1 defeat to Blackburn Rovers and West Ham head into Saturday's clash in desperate need of a victory.

Jordan Thompson's stunning strike proved the difference as Preston picked up their first win in seven attempts under new boss Graham Alexander.

The Lilywhites will be looking to build on that win by getting another positive result on Saturday and it is worth noting that they have won their last three league games against West Ham.

If Preston can pick up another victory, it will be the first time they have managed back-to-back league wins since January.

West Ham will be relying on Bowen as their main attacking threat, with support from striker Joel Piroe and fellow winger Edvin Austbo.

Arne Engels, Mohamadou Kante and Divine Mukasa are expected to continue in midfield.

Preston are likely to continue with the same side that beat Southampton, with Callum Lang set to lead the line.

Thompson and Andrew Moran will continue their midfield partnership, while Andrew Hughes, Liam Lindsay and Odeluga Offiah are set to line up in a back three.

Los Blancos actually have the joint-best defensive record in La Liga this season with only 10 conceded - the same as both Celta and Atletico Madrid.

Real Madrid will have revenge on their minds in this weekend's contest, having suffered a stunning 2-0 defeat to Celta in the corresponding match at Bernabeu last term.

The victory for Celta at Bernabeu last season proved to be their first win over Real Madrid since January 2017, while it was their first league success against Los Blancos since May 2014, with this fixture dominated by the capital giants throughout history.

Celta have been solid defensively this season but have found it incredibly difficult down the other end of the field, netting only 10 times in 12 league matches, which is among the worst attacking records in the division at this stage of proceedings.

That said, Claudio Giraldez's side did manage to put five unanswered goals past Salamanca in the first round of the Copa del Rey on November 13.

Celta's last league match ended in a goalless draw with Levante on November 8, and they currently sit 11th in the table, boasting a record of two wins, seven draws and three defeats.

The Sky Blues are also playing in the Europa League this season, currently sitting 27th in the overall table, picking up three points from their first four games of the campaign.

#2234. EFL club officially confirm they're going to reduce capacity of their stadium

Mansfield Town have made a decision to decrease the capacity of the One Call Stadium, otherwise known as Field Mill, after a meeting with the Safety Advisory Group.

The Stags are currently in the midst of a third successive campaign in League One, with Nigel Clough's side amassing 19 points from 12 matches.

As a result of the club's recent trajectory, Mansfield have welcomed several fallen giants to this part of Nottinghamshire.

Stags fans have developed a reputation for generating plenty of noise, which, in turn, has led to a strong atmosphere with those backing the visiting sides housed in the stadium's North Stand.

However, the decision, which has been made ahead of Saturday's clash with MK Dons, hasn't gone down well with several fans.

The One Call Stadium is currently the fifth-smallest stadium in League One, with only the home grounds of Cambridge United, Stevenage, Burton Albion and Bromley able to house fewer spectators.

Attendances so far this season have been at the 8,527 mark on average, but Mansfield will now see fewer visiting supporters pass through the turnstiles.

A statement from Nottinghamshire Council on Friday confirmed that the stadium's North Stand will be reduced in capacity from 1,000 to 600 following incidents relating to crowd management and control in the EFL Trophy clash against Chesterfield, which the League Two side won 5-3.

Mansfield have since confirmed the agreement in conjunction with the council and Safety Advisory Group, but also confirmed that the club are exploring the possibility of a safe-standing area within the stand.

Although this decision doesn't have a major impact on the matchday experience of the home support at the One Call Stadium, other than the fact there are fewer visiting fans to engage in back-and-forth with, it has caused plenty of responses.

One Stags supporter wrote: "SAG vs Mansfield Town yet again. Do they ever work with the club rather than against it?"

"But why? What was said in the meeting? Away fans getting punished by the SAG which reflects on the club? They deserve a bit of clarity," another commented.

"Poor decision and tbh, it’s rarely full," backed up the feeling of confusion as to why such measures have been taken.

Another Mansfield fan debated: "Surely (has) to be done up and down the country then?

"There is literally nothing wrong with away fans standing up. SAG need to give their head a wobble," was one of many strong reactions.

With Mansfield's last two home games coming against the Spireites and Notts County, another questioned: "Did SAG not look at who the last 3 games have been against at home? At least 2 they should’ve known it would’ve been an issue."

One of the potential fans visiting from Buckinghamshire on Saturday said: "What('s) wrong with standing up? No need to close off part of the stand because of it."

What do you make of Mansfield Town's decision to reduce the One Call Stadium's capacity? Comment below!

However, a Port Vale fan agreed with the decision, replying: "The ticket regulations say that fans should be sitting down. If they stand up and refuse to sit then have them thrown out. If they refuse to leave then have police arrest them. Don't punish other fans because there are some idiots."

Although Mansfield clarified that the allocation will be temporarily reduced, it has already been debated for how long this will be the case.

Recent fixtures have clearly impacted this decision, and some of League One's largest fanbases could be left scrambling for tickets depending on how long such measures are in place.

Indeed, the Stags are still yet to host the likes of Barnsley, Sheffield Wednesday and Leicester City, with it not exactly being an arduous journey for those who follow the Tykes, Owls and Foxes to make.

FLW have ranked the 10 best EFL League One stadiums so far in the 2026/27 season, including Bradford City and Sheffield Wednesday.

Ironically, the decision to house fewer away supporters could still play to the advantage of Clough's side, who have picked up the majority of their points so far on home turf.

#2232. Barnsley make Huddersfield Town ticket announcement and it's caused backlash

Barnsley have confirmed that adult tickets for their game against Huddersfield Town next month will cost £35.

The Tykes have had a tough season so far, with Daniel Stendel’s return to Oakwell not producing the results that many had hoped, as the team sit in the relegation zone.

However, many fans recognise that Barnsley’s problems run deeper right now, and there are calls for the owners to leave the club.

Whilst the criticism towards those at the top of the club is wide-ranging, one aspect that has left fans annoyed is the price it costs to watch games.

And, that has ignited again, with the club confirming that adult tickets for the home clash against Huddersfield on December 19 will be £35.

This was always going to be a game that was priced higher than most, as there is a rivalry between the two Yorkshire clubs - and it’s worth noting it’s the same pricing structure as the one they went with against Sheffield Wednesday.

Plus, with Huddersfield flying high, they are likely to shift a lot of tickets, so Barnsley, who will be charging the visitors £35 as well, will see this as a chance to make money.

Firstly, it should be said that problems with ticket prices are not exclusive to Barnsley, and fans of clubs up and down the country would want their own clubs to review prices.

But, in this instance, you can understand why Barnsley fans are furious, as £35 is an awful lot of money to watch a League One fixture.

Barnsley will be wincing at the latest news out of League One involving Bradford and a potential takeover development mirroring Sheffield Wednesday's.

Yes, it’s a game against a local side, but Barnsley are battling to stay in the third tier right now, so they should be doing all they can to bring supporters back, which isn’t going to happen when you’re charging £35.

Plus, this is a game that will be played a week before Christmas, at a time when money is tight for everyone. When you combine that with the fixture being televised, it really is a poor decision from Barnsley, who are prioritising money over fans.

Again, they’re by no means the only club to do that, but it’s a shame that they aren’t going the other way and trying to do affordable deals to entice supporters back.

Of course, the hierarchy will argue that they need to bring in funds to help the club, but it’s always about striking the right balance and making fans feel valued.

What do you make of Barnsley's ticket prices? Let us know in the comments below.

As you would expect, this development didn’t go down well with the Barnsley fans, with many making it clear that they feel this is far too much money to ask fans to pay.

However, there was one Barnsley fan who did understand the thinking of the Barnsley hierarchy, as he explained why it made 'commercial sense'.

Meanwhile, these Huddersfield fans couldn't resist having a dig at their rivals, as they joked that £35 was more than fair to get to watch Martin Drury's team, and one player in particular.

#2229. The West Ham teenager who stunned Arsenal is back after four years

Former West Ham United midfielder Reece Oxford has returned to English football, as he signed a deal with League One side, Bromley.

The midfielder first caught the eye with the Hammers in the early stages of his professional career, as he produced an eye-catching display in his senior debut against Arsenal in August 2015.

Oxford became West Ham's youngest-ever Premier League player at 16 years and 236 days old, and his performance looked as though he'd been playing regularly in the top-flight.

West Ham ran out 2-0 winners at the Emirates against Arsenal in that match, and Oxford came in for high praise from Sky Sports' co-commentator on the day, Niall Quinn.

“He’s been phenomenal. A star is born,” Sky Sports co-commentator Niall Quinn said. “He’s been mature, he’s ordering people around. It’s unbelievable to see. He’s mobile, he gets around and he knows the game. He’s a natural.”

Oxford went on to label his performance as a 'dream debut' in the win over the Gunners, and an 'honour' to play for West Ham in the top-flight.

“It was a dream debut,” Oxford told Sky Sports. “It’s an honour to play for West Ham and to be the youngest.”

Oxford is now 27-years-old, and it's fair to say that his career hasn't quite hit the heights that he and supporters would have originally hoped to see.

Whilst contracted to West Ham, he had loan spells with Reading whilst they were in the Championship, before two brief spells with German side Borussia Mönchengladbach, before having his final loan spell with FC Augsburg.

He seemingly made a good impression with Augsburg as well, as he went on to sign for them on a permanent basis in 2019. Oxford then spent six years in Germany with them, making 80 appearances in total across all competitions.

The former England youth international's time with Augsburg came to an end at the end of the 2024/25 season, when he departed when his contracted expired.

Can you see Reece Oxford's move to Bromley kickstarting his career again? Let us know in the comments!

The midfielder had a year out of competitive football, and it'll be interesting to see if he can recapture his best form, as he's a player with real quality when at his best, which we saw with both West Ham and Augsburg.

There will be some doubts whether he can do that though, as Oxford hasn't played a competitive match since November 2022, as he's had numerous injury problems which have stalled his development in senior football.

A move to Bromley could be the ideal move for him, although the League One side are struggling in their efforts to avoid relegation back into League Two.

Bromley are next in action on Saturday, when they travel to the Select Car Leasing Stadium to take on Reading, in what will be Oxford's first trip back to his former club.

Bromley have found it tough to build any sort of momentum this season, as they're bottom of the League One table, as well as being three points adrift of safety heading into this weekend's fixtures.

This dismal run of form includes being winless from their last 11 matches, and it's a tough ask for them to end that run against a Reading side that have only lost once at home so far in the 2026/27 season.

Oxford has a point to prove at this level, having not played this far down the English football pyramid, but if he can hit the ground running, then Bromley will have a great player on their hands.

#2228. Monaco summit adds unnecessary drama to Michael Carrick situation

Michael Carrick arrived at Carrington for Manchester United training as normal on Tuesday morning, while his bosses were preparing to decide his future in talks on the Mediterranean.

Chief executive Omar Berrada and director of football Jason Wilcox are flying to Monaco to meet Sir Jim Ratcliffe, with the BBC reporting that the pair will recommend Carrick is sacked.The Guardian has indicated representatives of the Glazer family will also be involved.

The club insists the Monaco summit was pre-planned and is not solely about the head coach and while that may be true, it hardly matters.By allowing the meeting to become public knowledge, United have turned a straightforward football decision into unnecessary drama, compounding an already difficult situation.

What does is say about Man United in 2026 that a manager is given three games to secure his job, then left to train the squad during an international break while the executives are flying off to rule on his future? It’s farcical.

The latest chapter is another muddled process.Wilcox and Berrada met at Carrington the day after the 3-0 home defeat to Bournemouth on 25 October and agreed to give Carrick three more matches.A defeat at Chelsea, a win over Roma and a 4-4 draw with Aston Villa later, they appear to have reached the conclusion many expected a fortnight ago.

If the timeline holds, the decision will be made at Wednesday’s meeting, with an official announcement expected later in the week.United reportedly want to tell Carrick face to face, which means he could spend another day or two working under a cloud. In reality, it could be two days or two weeks.

The stakes could hardly be higher. United have won once in 10 league games, their worst-ever start to a Premier League season, and sit one point off the bottom in 18th place.

Should Carrick go, attention will turn quickly to his successor.ESPN reports that United States head coach Mauricio Pochettino is the favoured candidate, while Eddie Howe has been named as Wilcox’s preferred choice. Antonio Conte has also been mentioned.

Carrick deserves better than this.Whatever the outcome, a club that wants to be taken seriously should not be settling its manager’s future in the full glare of the media while he is still out on the training pitch.

#2226. Doncaster Rovers can’t be blinded by Grant McCann evidence as decision looms

Grant McCann’s return to Doncaster Rovers has been an undoubted success, but the cracks are beginning to show.

Rovers have slumped into the relegation zone of League One, been embarrassed by non-league opposition in the FA Cup, and lacked the signs of a team who could improve.

McCann’s men are flailing. They have won just one of their most recent five league fixtures. That was an impressive victory against a sub-par Sheffield Wednesday side, who sacked Henrik Pedersen in the aftermath.

They have shipped six goals away to Milton Keynes Dons, three at Luton Town, and a further three at Kidderminster Harriers. The latter saw them exit from the FA Cup at the first time of asking, further worsening their away form.

In the league, Rovers have won just once away from home this season, losing five and drawing the other of their seven trips. Their xG does place them 16th in the table, but their xG conceded puts them in the relegation zone with poor defensive statistics in particular, according to FotMob.

League One relegation zone as of November 19

No one has scored more than three goals for them in league action so far this term, with a form in parallel to the run that was play-off worthy form after the closure of the January transfer window last term.

That is the key for McCann. The form of his Doncaster team in the second half of campaigns.

Rovers collected 30 points in the last 18 games of the 2025/26 season, with only five teams managing more. That included both automatically promoted sides, and two of the teams who finished inside the play-offs alongside one who landed just outside.

It was the same the prior season, with their form after the closure of the January transfer window better than everyone else in League Two on route to their title win.

That theme can be stretched even further back, with the 2024/25 season seeing Doncaster manage the best record of any team in the fourth tier between the beginning of February and end of the campaign. That included a perfect home record of eight wins from eight, and only a single loss away from the then Eco-Power Stadium.

That season may have ended in bitter disappointment, with a penalty shootout defeat to Crawley Town in the semi-finals of the play-offs, but it was the first of three consecutive campaigns where post-January results had fixed a problem caused by early-season woes.

This time around it feels different. Chunks of the Doncaster fanbase have turned on McCann, not for the first time, with pointed chants directed at the Rovers manager following their chastening loss at Kidderminister. The dressing room and then the boardroom could follow suit if results, and performances, do not turn in the right direction.

We rank all 24 League One stadiums by their ground's distance from the city or town, starting with the longest distance.

The fixture list has not been kind either, with high-flying trio Bradford City, Reading, and league-leaders Huddersfield Town the next three opponents for the struggling Doncaster. McCann needs to get his team creating more chances, finishing them at a more consistent rate, and defending in a more cohesive and structured manner at the opposite end of the pitch.

To expect a trend to continue for a fourth season in a row might be asking for one too many miracles. If underwhelming performances continue to stack up and points continue to be lost, then Rovers could end up in a hole that even the fortunes of McCann’s recent second-half of the season displays can not dig them out of.

#2225. “Quite simply disgusting”: Gary Neville tears into Manchester United over Carrick sacking

Graeme Hanna is the site lead of Read Rangers and also writes about West Ham for Read West Ham. A Rangers season-ticket holder and freelance writer, he has previously written for Vital Rangers, Rangers Review, Football Ground Guide and Give Me Sport, and has a long association with the Follow Follow fanzine.

Gary Neville has launched a furious attack on Manchester United’s leadership over the way the club handled Michael Carrick’s dismissal.

United confirmed on Thursday that Carrick had been sacked, four days after the 4-4 draw with Aston Villa left them 18th in the Premier League with eight points from 10 games.

The club said it had reached the “extremely difficult conclusion that a change is necessary”.

Neville had already branded the process “classless” on The Overlap’s Stick to Football podcast before the announcement, pointing out that it was widely known Carrick was about to go while United were holding talks with potential replacements.

Speaking to Sky Sports after the decision was confirmed, the former United captain went even further.

“It’s just quite simply disgusting,” he said.

“That falls well below the standard of what you would expect in any other business, in any other industry. It would be constructive dismissal and you’d be getting sued.”

Neville took particular aim at the decision to hold the crucial meeting in Monaco, with chief executive Omar Berrada and director of football Jason Wilcox flying out to meet Sir Jim Ratcliffe while Carrick was still taking training at Carrington.

“The optics of being in Monaco whilst the team, the staff, the people who are running the club on a day-to-day basis are in Manchester, it’s just not good and it reflects badly on the leadership,” he said.

It seems the only ones not to realise that are the trio above, but they aren’t stupid. How they explain it, or attempt to rationalise, it will be painful to listen to.

Neville also argued the club should have acted sooner. Looking back to the 3-0 home defeat to Bournemouth in October, he said United “effectively watched Michael Carrick resign on the pitch” as he waved goodbye to supporters at full time, only for the board to hand him three more games.

He summed up the episode as “indecision, lack of authority, just basically really poor leadership”.

It’s more than an episode, it’s an omnishambles that reflects the dysfunctional state of MUFC in its current guise.

United are now expected to move quickly for Mauricio Pochettino. The United States head coach is reported to have reached an agreement in principle with the club, which is prepared to pay his release clause.

First, they will need to wait until the USMNT completes its CONCACAF Nations League quarter-final double header against Haiti on Saturday and Wednesday.

Yes. Man United to wait until an international break to appoint an international manager…

For Neville, though, the bigger problem lies above the dugout and no manager can be the scapegoat for that massive issue.

#2224. Motherwell vs Rangers - prediction, team news, lineups

By Calum Burrowes | 19 Nov 2026 12:39, Last updated: 19 Nov 2026 12:46

Returning to Scottish Premiership action for the first time in two weeks, Motherwell welcome an in-form Rangers side to Fir Park on Saturday afternoon.

The Well head into the fixture following a 1-1 draw away to Dundee United, while the Gers extended their unbeaten run in all competitions to 12 matches with a 2-0 victory over St Mirren, leaving them four points behind league leaders Celtic with a game in hand.

After an impressive fourth-placed finish in the Scottish Premiership last season under Jens Berthel Askou, Motherwell secured European football and earned widespread praise for their attacking style of play.

However, Askou left Fir Park over the summer to take charge of Toulouse, with the North Lanarkshire club appointing Swedish coach Alfred Johansson as his replacement, although he has so far struggled to build on the progress made during the 2025-26 campaign.

After 13 Scottish Premiership matches, the Well sit ninth in the table with three wins, four draws and six defeats, having conceded 21 goals, with only three sides shipping more, while just three teams have scored fewer than their 14.

Despite their worrying start to the new season, Motherwell entered the final international break of the year on the back of a three-match unbeaten run, with draws against St Johnstone and Dundee United either side of a narrow 1-0 victory over St Mirren on Halloween.

While that positive end to October and start to November has offered some encouragement to Johansson's side, they will know they must pick up where they left off before the two-week break to create more breathing room above the bottom two and close the gap on the teams higher up the table.

As for Rangers, they appear to have put their difficult start under Derek McInnes behind them, with the Gers heading into the busy festive period in a strong position to challenge for silverware and close in on rivals Celtic in the Premiership title race.

McInnes's side entered the break with a 2-0 away victory over St Mirren, courtesy of goals either side of half-time from Ryan Naderi and Dan Neil, extending their unbeaten run to 12 matches and marking their first defeat-free spell since August, which has also included two victories over their bitter Glasgow rivals.

This impressive turnaround has followed an early European exit and a difficult start to their domestic campaign under the former Hearts boss, but Rangers are now showing significant signs of improvement compared to recent seasons.

At this stage last year, the Gers were fourth in the table and had recently replaced Russell Martin with Danny Rohl, following another difficult managerial spell, while they had also been sitting third 12 months earlier, highlighting the progress McInnes has made since taking charge.

Despite their recent resurgence, Rangers remain four points behind Celtic, although a victory on Saturday would cut that gap to a single point, with a game in hand on the league leaders still to come.

The sides meet for the second time this season following Rangers' narrow 1-0 victory at Ibrox in early September, with the Gers having since turned their home ground into something of a fortress, as no side has taken a point off them there since their defeat on matchweek two.

Motherwell could name the same starting XI that drew 1-1 with Dundee United, although they remain without several first-team players through injury.

Alex Lowry, Johnny Koutroumbis and Dylan Williams are all expected to miss out due to knee, muscle and hamstring problems respectively, although the trio could return by the start of December.

Lukas Fadinger has scored twice in his last two appearances, albeit both goals coming from the penalty spot, and is expected to anchor the midfield while wearing the captain's armband once again.

Meanwhile, the visitors make the short journey south with a longer list of injury concerns, although they at least avoided any fresh setbacks during their victory over St Mirren.

McInnes will remain without Ross McCrorie, Cameron Devlin, Daisuke Yokota, Lawrence Shankland, Youssef Chermiti and Jose Cifuentes, although Chermiti is the only one of the injured players expected to remain sidelined into 2027.

#2223. BREAKING: Mauricio Pochettino agrees Manchester United deal to become new manager

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United have confirmed an agreement with Mauricio Pochettino to become their next head coach, with the Argentine set to take charge after the international break.

The 54-year-old will replace Michael Carrick, who was dismissed on Thursday following United’s disastrous start to the Premier League season. Pochettino is set to sign a two-and-a-half-year contract that will keep him at Old Trafford until the summer of 2029.

The former Tottenham and Chelsea boss is currently managing the United States national team but is expected to fly straight to Manchester after completing his USA international games.

Pochettino will first oversee the USA’s CONCACAF Nations League quarter-final ties against Haiti on Saturday and next Tuesday. He is expected to arrive in Manchester on Wednesday before taking charge of the club on Thursday.

Pochettino’s first taste of action as a United manager will be against Liverpool at Anfield on November 22.

Pochettino brings considerable Premier League experience after previous spells with Southampton, Tottenham and Chelsea. He has also managed Paris Saint-Germain before the USA job.

His appointment makes him Manchester United’s eighth permanent manager since Sir Alex Ferguson retired in 2013.

Carrick’s departure came less than six months after he signed a two-year contract. His second spell in charge ended after United managed just one win in their opening 10 league matches, leaving them in the relegation zone.

Carrick’s coaching staff, including Jonny Evans, Jonathan Woodgate, Steve Holland, Travis Binnion and goalkeeping coach Craig Mawson, have all left alongside Carrick.

#2221. QPR release new Adel Taarabt footage and it's getting fans emotional

Queens Park Rangers have released video footage of modern-day cult hero Adel Taarabt returning to Loftus Road, and given his status in West London, supporters have been reacting in their droves.

The Moroccan is highly-regarded as one of the silkiest players to ever grace the Championship in its modern era, with his influence helping Rangers achieve promotion to the Premier League in 2010/11 under the charismatic Neil Warnock.

Throughout his career, which included spells at some of Europe's most prestigious clubs, the playmaker often made headlines for both good and bad reasons, none of which tainted his legacy in W12.

Rangers are looking to end a long-standing exile from the top-flight in the near future under Julien Stephan, who has worked with some of the game's current leading lights such as Desire Doue and 2025 Ballon d'Or winner, Ousmane Dembélé.

Hoops supporters have certainly got their favourites within Stephan's side right now, but for a brief moment, the current situation in this part of the capital has been forgotten about as Taarabt made an emotional return to his former stomping ground.

Taarabt, 37, announced his retirement from professional football two months ago, with it being well over a decade since his chaotic spell in West London came to an end.

After coming through the RC Lens academy, the 30-time Moroccan international spent time with Tottenham Hotspur before initially joining Rangers on loan in March 2009, before also spending the following season on loan with the club.

Despite being told a permanent signing of Taarabt would see him sacked, Warnock made the playmaker's move permanent in August 2010, with the decision proving to be a stroke of genius as the R's won the league and the cult figure simultaneously enjoyed one of the best individual seasons in Championship history with 19 goals and 21 assists in 44 appearances.

The remainder of Taarabt's time in W12 was certainly less smooth, including public spats with Harry Redknapp, before he departed for Benfica in June 2015 after 164 outings for the club.

Earlier in the week, the former ace teased on Instagram that he had returned to London, but clearly kept the reasoning secret with QPR posting a short clip titled: "The King is Home," with Taarabt emerging from the Loftus Road tunnel onto the pitch.

11 years since his departure, Hoops fans are clearly not in a rush to forget Taarabt's magic which graced the Loftus Road turf on multiple occasions.

Hundreds of them have been reacting on social media to the clip, with one saying: "There is just something about seeing a player back at the stadium, where he gave you some of the best moments supporting this wonderful club."

Another supporter responded: "Why is this emotional! Can tell how much of an impact he had, just seeing him on that pitch again is insane."

"I hope the club is bringing him back as a legend," wrote another Rangers fan.

One of several overjoyed supporters also said: "One of my favourite players of all time and maybe a pain to play with but a bigger pain to play against. One if not the best player to play in the Championship ever,off the pitch a nightmare on it a joy to watch."

"Anyone know why he is here? He’s turned up when we have back to back away games….What does it all mean??," another fan debated.

Given the skillset Taarabt possessed, yet another comment read: Surely he can still play??," whilst another fan jokingly claimed: "Put him in front of Madsen and Kamara and we probably win the league."

FLW ranks the eight most famous footballers to have played in the Championship at some point in their career.

As previously mentioned, it is unclear what brought Taarabt back to Rangers' historic home, even though it is no surprise that it has brought back many memories.

Some supporters have attempted to put two and two together, though, after it was recently announced that former Championship goalscoring icon, Charlie Austin, will be present at the club's home clash with Sheffield United next weekend.

Rangers fans, where does Adel Taarabt rank in your all-time list of Loftus Road greats? Comment below!

Given Austin's similar impact on the Hoops' dramatic 2013/14 play-off-winning season, the former striker is sure to get a heroes' welcome, but if a return in front of fans was arranged with Taarabt, excitement would definitely be off the charts.

Mauricio Pochettino officially begins work as United head coach on Wednesday, just four days before his first match against Liverpool at Anfield.

The 54-year-old replaces Michael Carrick, who was dismissed last week after United won just one of their opening 10 Premier League matches, leaving them 18th in the table.

Pochettino arrives after completing his final assignment with the United States, who beat Haiti 2-0 on Tuesday to secure an 8-0 aggregate victory in their CONCACAF Nations League quarter-final.

Speaking after his final match with the United States, Pochettino acknowledged the challenge awaiting him in Manchester.

“The new challenge is a big challenge, but at the same time it’s adrenaline you want to feel,” he said.

Several members of his coaching staff, including long-time assistant Jesus Perez, travelled to Manchester earlier this week to begin preparations. Pochettino will have limited time to work with his squad before Sunday’s Premier League meeting with Liverpool, which kicks off at 4.30pm GMT.

The former Tottenham, Chelsea and Paris Saint-Germain manager inherits a United side struggling for results after a difficult opening to the campaign. He previously guided Tottenham to the 2019 Champions League final and won the French league title during his spell with Paris Saint-Germain.

United’s next fixtures also include a Champions League trip to Sporting CP and a home Premier League match against Brentford. Pochettino’s immediate task is to improve United’s league position, beginning with a demanding first match at Anfield.

#2219. Liverpool: Why Brajan Gruda would be perfect Mo Salah successor

By Axel Clody | 19 Nov 2026 12:41

Brajan Gruda has been one of the revelations of the start of the season with Leipzig and Germany. His profile appears ideal to strengthen Liverpool’s attack.

An attacking midfielder by training, Brajan Gruda is shining at the start of the season as a right winger. After a disappointing spell at Brighton, the 22-year-old German bounced back last winter at RB Leipzig. He has been loaned this season by the English club to the German side. Leipzig will sign him permanently in 2027 for £25.5m (€30m). Gruda will then receive a contract until June 2031.

That transfer does not mean he will stay long at Leipzig, however. According to Sky Sport Germany, Bayern Munich have the Germany international on their shortlist. The Bavarian club have not planned any move in this case, unless Michael Olise were to leave. It must be said that, all things considered, the profiles of the two players are quite similar.

Beyond the fact that they are left-footed number 10s shining on the right side of the attack, they are technical players who are rather fond of assists. Brajan Gruda is the fourth player in Europe’s top five leagues with the most successful dribbles at the start of this season, with 36.

He is very efficient in this area, succeeding with 70.6% of his dribbles, a better ratio than Lamine Yamal (58.3%) or Kylian Mbappe (59.7%), who nevertheless attempt more. Beyond dribbles, the 22-year-old is also decisive, with two goals scored and five assists delivered in 13 games for Leipzig.

Those performances opened the doors of the Germany national team. Jurgen Klopp did not hesitate to give him his first two caps, starting him against Serbia (3-1 win) and the Netherlands (5-0) during this November international break. Gruda shone with a solo goal against the Serbs, as well as another goal and two assists against the Dutch.

His feat against Serbia impressed his manager. ‘It’s completely crazy. The defender in front of him? Poor guy!’ - the German manager exclaimed.

On the pitch, in those two games, his understanding with Florian Wirtz, playing as number 10, was also highlighted. Could the two men therefore be reunited at Liverpool?

Liverpool did not sign a genuine right winger this summer, after the departure of Mohamed Salah. Left-footed, technical and provocative, Brajan Gruda has an interesting profile to replace the Egyptian star. He is capable of long solo runs and of shining in tight spaces, relying on his silky left foot. He is a player who has emerged in recent months.

Liverpool therefore seem to have every interest in quickly getting their hands on the Leipzig player. Indeed, if he continues to perform at this level, his market value, which stands at £23.8m (€28m), should rise considerably, while new European heavyweights, in addition to Bayern Munich, will take an interest in his profile.

In this case, Liverpool have two allies who could push Brajan Gruda to join the English giants: Florian Wirtz and Jurgen Klopp.

#2218. ‘Sometimes to love is to let go’, former Man Utd midfielder announces retirement

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Former Manchester United midfielder Juan Mata has announced his retirement from professional football at the age of 38, bringing an end to a beautiful career of around 20 years.

The Spaniard went on to win one of the biggest trophies in professional football. The former Manchester United midfielder confirmed his decision on Wednesday after ending his playing career with Australian side Melbourne Victory.

Mata enjoyed a memorable spell at Manchester United after arriving from Chelsea in January 2014. He spent more than eight years at Old Trafford, making 285 appearances, scoring 51 goals and winning four trophies.

His first trophy with Manchester United came in the 2016 FA Cup final against Crystal Palace. He also helped the club win the Community Shield, League Cup and Europa League during the 2016/17 campaign.

Before moving to Manchester, Juan Mata had established himself as one of Chelsea’s most creative players. He won the club’s Player of the Year award in both of his first two full seasons at Stamford Bridge before eventually joining United as David Moyes’ marquee signing.

Mata probably had the biggest moments of his career in the Spain shirt. He earned 41 international caps and scored 10 goals, becoming part of the most talented Spanish team in history. He went on to win the 2010 FIFA World Cup and Euro 2012 with Spain while etching his name in Spanish football history with a goal in Spain’s 4-0 victory over Italy in the European Championship final.

After leaving Manchester United in 2022, the midfielder continued his career with Galatasaray, Vissel Kobe and Western Sydney Wanderers before joining Melbourne Victory in September 2025.

His final season in Australia was an impressive one. Mata registered five goals and 13 assists, won the Johnny Warren Medal and earned a place in the A-League Team of the Season.

Reflecting on his decision, Mata spoke about the reason that kept him playing for so many years.

“I was able to keep going for so many years because of the sheer joy of playing,” he said.

He also made it clear that retirement would not mean walking away from football altogether.

“Football will never leave my life. There are so many ways to continue loving the game.”

Manchester United also paid tribute to their former midfielder, writing on social media: “Great player, better person. Thank you for representing our colours with class, Juan.”

#2217. Shock Tom Brady, Chelsea twist emerges after Birmingham City exit

The seven-times winning NFL star Tom Brady divested his interest in Birmingham City just a couple of weeks ago, but it's now being reported that he's already set to re-enter English football.

The high-profile involvement of former NFL quarterback Tom Brady was a real headline-grabber, when the Knighthead Capital group, headed by Tom Wagner, took control of Birmingham City in 2023.

Over the three and a half years since then, the Blues have been on quite a rollercoaster ride. They were relegated into League One at the end of their first season in charge, but then bounced back as champions with a record-breaking 111 points.

Last season saw Birmingham finish in 10th place in the Championship, but as clubs return from the November international break they sit at the top of the division ahead of Middlesbrough and Stoke City.

But if Birmingham are going to end this season by returning to the Premier League following an absence of 16 years, they'll be doing so without Brady, who relinquished his shareholding in the club earlier in November. But Brady's interest in investing in football doesn't seem to have gone anywhere, with fresh reporting now suggesting that he could be set to buy a small stake in Premier League giants Chelsea.

The Guardian reports on Friday afternoon that Tom Brady is in negotiations with Chelsea over buying a small stake in the Premier League club. Sources have told them that this is intended to "to pave the way for investing at Stamford Bridge."

Brady is understood to have a good relationship with Behdad Eghbali, whose fund Clearlake Capital own 86.5% of Chelsea after former co-investors Todd Boehly and Mark Walter sold their shares in the London club in September.

How do you feel about Tom Brady's potential involvement at Stamford Bridge? Let us know in the comments below!

When Brady sold up his stake in Birmingham City, the club issued a statement thanking him for his involvement at their club: "Birmingham City Football Club expresses its sincere gratitude to Tom Brady for his involvement with and unwavering support for the Club over the last three years."

The club went on to add, "It was truly amazing to have someone with Tom’s experience and passion associated with the Club, and the Club looks forward to continuing to build on that foundation from the training ground to the Birmingham community."

The NFL icon severed his ties with the Championship leaders on Monday evening

The departure of Tom Brady comes at the start of what could turn out to be the most exciting period in the history of Birmingham City. The club unveiled their plans for a new 62,000-capacity stadium as part of a 'sports quarter' in November 2025, and the club hope to be in their new home for the start of the 2030-31 season.

And things are getting interesting for the team, as well. Going into the international break, a run of six wins in seven games had lifted Chris Davies' team to top spot in the Championship, a symbolic sign of their ongoing progress since Knighthead took ownership of them, just over three years ago.

Chelsea have refused to comment on this story, but the appeal to Brady isn't a huge surprise, given the events of the summer of 2025. When Chelsea won the FIFA Club World Cup in July of that year, he was reported to have joined the Chelsea squad for a private party to celebrate their win.

While involved with Birmingham, Brady was appointed the chair of their new advisory board, and his time there wasn't without controversy. He earned headlines after criticising former manager Wayne Rooney, with the former England star replying to the criticism by saying that, "I don’t think he really understood football."

Brady's involvement at St Andrew's ensured huge publicity for the club at the time of the takeover, but his actual shareholding in the club was only 3.3%. With the team top of the Championship table and the planning and infrastructure work continuing apace for the new stadium, Birmingham City supporters should be able to rest easy in the knowledge that his departure from their club is unlikely to temper the ambition of the club's majority owners.

Edwards is regarded as one of the most influential executives in modern English football, having played a major role in building Liverpool’s squad under Jurgen Klopp.

The 54-year-old has built a close working relationship with his coaching team throughout spells at Espanyol, Southampton, Tottenham, Paris Saint-Germain and Chelsea. Several of those staff members have followed him to Manchester United.

Jesus Perez is Pochettino’s long-time assistant and is widely regarded as his right-hand man. Their relationship began at Espanyol, where Perez initially worked as an analyst before becoming his assistant at Southampton. He has followed the Argentine throughout his career with spells at Tottenham, PSG and USA. He is now set to take the same role at Old Trafford.

Jimenez will work as United’s goalkeeping coach. He first joined Pochettino’s coaching setup at Espanyol in 2011 and has remained alongside him throughout his managerial career, including their time in England, France and the United States.

The manager’s 31-year-old son has also joined the coaching group. Sebastiano started working with his father during their time at Tottenham and has continued as a sports scientist and fitness coach with him with the USA team. He is also joining Pochettino at Manchester United.

The former Argentine defender has worked with Pochettino since his first managerial job at Espanyol. He has been involved in training sessions, video analysis and tactical preparation, helping the manager develop game plans.

The arrivals mean a major change behind the scenes at Carrington following the departures of Michael Carrick and his coaching staff.

Former Swansea defender Ashley Williams is expected to join Leon Britton and Kris O'Leary in an interim coaching group on Saturday afternoon, when the hosts will look to extend their unbeaten home run against West Brom to eight matches.

West Brom suffered a major fitness blow during the two-week break, with influential midfielder Ousmane Diakite picking up a calf injury whilst on duty for Mali.

The much-improved 26-year-old faces at least eight weeks on the sidelines, joining fellow midfielder Toby Collyer in the medical room.

The Baggies are also short on options in defence, with Noah Galves (knee) out of action, but captain George Campbell is available for selection.

#2213. Is Mauricio Pochettino’s job at Manchester United the toughest in the Premier League?

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United hired Mauricio Pochettino as their next manager to replace Michael Carrick, who managed just one win in 10 games.

His appointment makes him Manchester United’s eighth permanent manager since Sir Alex Ferguson retired in 2013.

Pochettino brings considerable Premier League experience after previous spells with Southampton, Tottenham and Chelsea. He has also managed Paris Saint-Germain before the USA job.

As he faces Liverpool for his first game on Sunday, the question arises: is he facing the toughest job in the Premier League?

The question was posed on BBC Radio about who is going to have more pressure, Pochettino or Thomas Frank at Crystal Palace.

According to former Premier League defender Nedum Onuoha, the expectations surrounding Manchester United will make the Argentine’s job considerably harder.

Pochettino is preparing for his first game in charge of Manchester United, with Liverpool waiting at Anfield on November 22. However, the challenge facing the new manager goes beyond getting results on the pitch.

Onuoha believes Pochettino will have to deal with constant discussion and criticism about his work, much of it coming from former United players who are now in the media.

Choosing between Pochettino and Frank, Onuoha said, “Probably Pochettino, because every former Manchester United player works in the media, so there will always be discussion around what he is doing.

“Whereas Frank can just enjoy playing his football at Crystal Palace. If he does well then it is great, if he doesn’t do well then it is still OK.

“But from Pochettino’s perspective, if Manchester United finish the season 15th in the Premier League, whether we like it or not, there will be some criticism of him because he’s had 28 games to do something.”

The comments just highlight the difference in expectations at the two clubs. Frank may have more freedom to work without the same level of attention, while Pochettino will be judged from day one against the standards historically associated with Manchester United. That is one big problem for Pochettino.

Rory Smith from The Observer highlighted another glaring problem at United.  Highlighting a major problem, Smith said, “there is a cycle element to Manchester United whereby a manager comes in, makes things a bit better, and then within a year to 18 months they aren’t challenging for titles again because the Premier League has changed since they were a dominant force.”

Pochettino’s first taste of action as a United manager will be against Liverpool at Anfield on November 22. That opening fixture will give him an immediate test, but the bigger task will be to get back United to winning ways, step by step.

Will the Manchester United job be without pressure for Mauricio Pochettino? Absolutely Not.

#2212. Blackburn Rovers will lick their lips at one Burnley situation - ‘it’s a disgrace’

After the final international break of 2026, Championship action is back on the agenda, with the weekend's round of fixtures ending with one of the division's spiciest encounters.

Indeed, the East Lancashire derby is back for the first time in two seasons, with general Championship observers likely to be tuning into the occasion which matters most to fans of Burnley and Blackburn Rovers.

It has been the Clarets who have enjoyed the majority of recent success in this part of the world, but Rovers fans will now feel it is now their time to receive the limelight.

Blackburn have already caused issues for some of the division's pre-season promotion candidates, but they will also be aware of the fact this is the best opportunity the club has had to get one over on Burnley for many years.

Within the past decade or so, the environment fostered by Burnley's players and supporters, particularly during the Sean Dyche era, made Turf Moor one of the most difficult stadiums for opponents to play at.

Even during the Clarets' recent past, many sides still found it difficult to leave with all three points, with none at all doing so during the 2024/25 season, in which Scott Parker's side conceded just 16 goals.

However, the recent demise in BB10 has been echoed by a new unwanted record set by the club - the longest period without a home league victory.

Burnley's last success at Turf Moor came on October 18th, 2025, in a 2-0 win over Leeds United, which will have been exactly 400 days by the time Sunday's local feud comes around.

They came agonisingly close to ensuring the record didn't stretch into a 12th month before Mohammed Fuseini's late equaliser for Derby County on September 19th, which prompted this reaction from the 'From the Bee Hole End' podcast.

"We are now (almost) a year without a win at Turf Moor. That's just incredible.

Rovers are currently positioned nine places and 11 points ahead of Burnley in the table - a feat not many would have predicted in pre-season.

Blackburn have certainly upset the apple cart on occasions this season, having defeated Middlesbrough and West Ham United 2-1 at Ewood Park, whilst also narrowly missing out on a victory by the same scoreline against Wolves at Molineux.

The Blues are only three points behind the play-off places after 15 games as a result, with Andri Gudjohnsen's 10-goal exploits, in particular, key to such a return.

Ahead of this particular grudge match, the squad will know that results on the road must improve if a top-eight bid is to be sustained, having taken just six points from a possible 21.

We rank the eight fiercest EFL rivalries ahead of Burnley vs Blackburn.

Whilst the current situation surrounding Fabio Celestini's side, who surrendered a 4-1 lead to lose 5-4 at Cardiff City last time out, will offer plenty of encouragement to those of a Blackburn persuasion, some may be pessimistic due to recent history.

During a period of Clarets-centred dominance in East Lancashire from 2009 to now, Sunday's hosts have not lost in the derby since March 2010, when a David Dunn penalty saw Blackburn achieve a league double over their bitter rivals.

This run stretches back 11 games, with the most high-profile of those coming in April 2023, when Manuel Benson's late curler secured the Championship title at Ewood Park under Vincent Kompany.

It is often debated that players do not need any added incentives to win games of such magnitude, but the news that Tony Mowbray has taken a step back from his role as Blackburn boss for health reasons will only give Rovers' players extra motivation.

They will be led by Damien Johnson and Mike Dodds in the meantime, although both have experience in the dugout on an interim or full-time basis.

Mowbray never took charge of this particular derby in his first spell at Ewood Park, with there even being a two-division gap between the two clubs in 2017/18.

#2211. Manchester United consider legal action over JJ Gabriel departure

Gary Gowers is a sports journalist and the editor of Read Everton, where he leads daily coverage of Everton news, transfers and analysis. He also writes about Manchester City for Read Man City and motorsport for Read Motorsport. A former accountant, he became a freelance writer in 2011 and has written for BBC Sport and Metro.

Manchester United are prepared to consider legal action if evidence emerges that another club approached academy forward JJ Gabriel without permission.

The 16-year-old announced his departure from Old Trafford in a social media video on Tuesday and is expected to join Real Madrid in January. According to The Times and ESPN, United remain concerned about the circumstances surrounding his proposed move, although no breach of transfer regulations has been established.

Gabriel has been absent from United since September, when he requested the cancellation of his registration. The teenager remains registered with the club, and United could explore legal or regulatory options if evidence of an unauthorised approach emerges.

The Independent reports that United do not want to punish Gabriel for actions they believe may have been taken by adults involved in his future. Real Madrid have been widely linked with the forward, but his transfer has not been formally completed.

Gabriel joined United’s academy in 2020 and was named the club’s young player of the year last season. He featured for the senior side during pre-season but has not made a competitive first-team appearance.

The youngster is eligible for a move within the European Union under an exception to FIFA’s rules on international transfers involving players aged under 18. In his farewell message, Gabriel thanked United for helping him develop during his six years at the club.

His expected move to Madrid would bring that association to an end, although questions remain over the process leading to his departure. United have not announced legal proceedings, and there has been no finding of wrongdoing by Real Madrid or any other club.

Manchester United have been linked with Como midfielder Nico Paz, although Real Madrid’s buy-back option and the absence of reported negotiations leave considerable uncertainty over a potential move.

The 22-year-old Argentina international has impressed in Italy this season, scoring six goals and providing eight assists in 14 appearances. According to The Sun, United have been monitoring Paz ahead of a possible summer transfer, with Mauricio Pochettino’s appointment potentially helping their chances.

Paz joined Como from Real Madrid in 2024 and has become an important player under Cesc Fabregas. He scored twice and provided an assist in Como’s 4-1 Champions League victory over United last month.

However, Madrid confirmed in June that they retain an option to re-sign the midfielder for the 2027-28 season. The clause is reportedly worth €80m (£67.6m), although the Spanish club have not publicly confirmed that figure.

Transfer journalist Fabrizio Romano has questioned the strength of United’s reported interest, saying there has been no contact or serious negotiation. Romano explained that the link had emerged because the same agency represents Paz and Pochettino.

He also suggested that Madrid remain central to the player’s future and that a January move is not being considered. United could assess their attacking midfield options next summer, with captain Bruno Fernandes’ contract situation still unresolved.

For now, Paz remains a reported target rather than a player involved in active transfer talks.

#2209. Sevilla vs Real Betis - prediction, team news, lineups

By Matt Law | 19 Nov 2026 13:21

The latest instalment of the Seville derby will take place at Estadio Ramon on Saturday evening, as Sevilla and Real Betis lock horns in La Liga.

Sevilla have historically dominated this fixture, but Real Betis have had the better of the recent meetings between the two, losing just one of their last eight contests with the White and Reds.

Sevilla have a record of five wins, three draws and four defeats from their 12 league matches this season, with 18 points leaving them in sixth position in the La Liga table.

Luis Garcia Plaza's side have only picked up one point from their last two league games, though, losing at Getafe at the start of November before drawing 0-0 with Alaves in their last fixture in front of their own fans before the international break.

Los Nervionenses have not managed to finish fourth in Spain's top flight since 2021-22, and there is no getting away from the fact that it has been a difficult few seasons for the club, finishing 12th, 14th, 17th and 13th in their last four campaigns.

However, Sevilla are currently showing form that could see them claim a European finish, which would be a major step forward given their recent troubles.

The White and Reds have won 61, lost 39 and drawn 35 of their previous 135 matches against Real Betis in all competitions, with their last success over their Seville rivals coming at home in October 2024.

Real Betis have claimed seven points from the last three league matches against Sevilla, including a 2-0 victory at Estadio Ramon last season, while they have lost just once to their rivals since February 2022.

Manuel Pellegrini's side recorded a 2-0 win over Elche in their last league match on November 8, before beating Navalcarnero 3-2 in the Copa del Rey four days later.

El Glorioso have won eight, drawn two and lost two of their 12 league matches this season to collect 26 points, which has left them in fourth spot in the division, eight points ahead of Sevilla leading into this weekend's Seville derby.

It has been a busy opening few months of the season for Real Betis given that Pellegrini's team are also playing in the Champions League; a strong start to their league stage has left them 11th in the table, claiming nine points from four matches.

The Seville team finished fifth in Spain's top flight last season and will again be expecting to challenge for a spot high up the division this term.

Sevilla will welcome Manu Bueno back into their squad on Saturday following a suspension, but Marcao, Peque and Miguel Sierra are again likely to miss out through injury.

Ruben Vargas is also a doubt due to the issue that forced him off against Alaves, which could mean that Chidera Ejuke is introduced into the starting side.

Robbie Ure is pushing to be introduced at centre-forward, but a tough start to his career with Sevilla has seen him manage just one goal in 12 league appearances.

Real Betis, meanwhile, remain without the services of Facundo Bernal through suspension.

Diego Llorente and Cucho Hernandez are also major doubts for the visitors on Saturday.

Troy Parrott is expected to continue through the middle for the away side, while Isco and Antony are also likely to retain their positions in the first XI.
